#+title: m46 linux readmen — two distros walk the README 0–6 under witness, logs inlined
#+filetags: :readme:docs:ghost:ledger:instance:init:kajuit:rolldown:musl:alpine:ubuntu:docker:qa:
#+status: WALKED. ubuntu 24.04 → step 6 reached, hello-world UP, 0 FAIL / 7 steps (5 PASS-WITH-DEVIATION). alpine 3.20 → step 6 reached, hello-world UP, step 4 FAIL verbatim (rolldown demands the musl napi binding under a gnu Deno) → PASS-WITH-DEVIATION after one env var; the README's Deno installer itself is unrunnable on musl.
#+status: Fixes: L1 APPLIED (README §0 names curl/ca-certificates/unzip + one apt line; 50.01:22 repeats the old claim, owed — emacs autosave present). Every other blast row is a proposal awaiting a per-item `go`. Both containers left RUNNING for inspection.
#+status: L4 RE-PINNED to a repo cause: vite 6's config bundler hands kajuit's config vite 8.1.0 + rolldown (measured in the ubuntu container). Two taskbags below: ghost process management (B1–B13) · the use/--instance cluster (C1–C12).
#+phase: wip (derived)
#+progress:  17% 3/18 (derived)
#+next: drain (derived)

* intent

beef, verbatim: /"ok. pull another linux distro and run the readme 0-6 again step by step. capture the
logs for either and write an quest/m46-linux-readmen.org file with the logs inlined. actually run 2.
subagents. one for ubuntu another for some small distro. pull readme from
github.com/vivalence/vivalence"/ · /"both subagents claude opus"/

One sentence: *the README Quickstart is a contract with a stranger's machine* — this quest records
two strangers (glibc Ubuntu 24.04, musl Alpine 3.20) taking steps 0–6 literally, with every byte
they saw inlined, so each defect below is a reproduction and not a recollection.

* terrain — measured

- *source*: GitHub =master= at =b27e0e62e2cde2ba5d80eaeed16d62db9de6c3f9= ("fix docs", crackedbeefcake).
  Local =git= HEAD is =2111b81ac= — GitHub is AHEAD: beef's colocated push already carries the fixes
  from =the-ubuntu-walk-lands-both-users-in-the-identity-table…= (=instance/lighthouse= rename, ghost
  exit 1 + one-line voice, headless =ledger/init=, =Instance.home=). Both walks therefore exercise
  the FIXED ghost — and both confirm those four fixes hold (see "verified fixed" below).
  =README.md= sha256 =c9cb142434302783553e296000c82d5d10b1527940ef57d6e90ad6c2170a9132=, 268 lines,
  byte-identical between GitHub, the local working tree, and both container clones.
- *host*: docker 26.1.1 in a linuxkit VM, =aarch64=. Containers =readmen-ubuntu= and =readmen-alpine=,
  NO published ports (curl runs inside via =docker exec=), both left RUNNING with =hello-world= up.
  The previous walk's =readmen= (ubuntu, socat → host =:1794= / =:2501=) is also still up.
- *walkers*: two opus subagents, one brief modulo distro (reproduced under =* the harness=). Each wrote
  its own =stepN.sh= / =.exp= scripts, ran them with =docker exec=, kept one log per step, and wrote
  =report.org= — those reports are inlined VERBATIM below, then the scripts, then the logs.
  Ubuntu: 72 tool calls, 32 min. Alpine: 140 tool calls, 29 min (six of them in the musl ladder).
- *prior art*: a single Ubuntu walk on =2111b81ac= (pre-fix) — compact
  =the-ubuntu-walk-lands-both-users-in-the-identity-table-registry-json-keeps-slugs-for-remote-taps-and-every-ghost-error-exits-zero=.

** verified fixed (both distros, on b27e0e62e)

| ubuntu-walk defect                    | evidence now                                                                                          |
|---------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| naked =instance/run= exit 0 + stack   | =ghost: instance: nothing selected — viva instances/use <slug>, --instance=<slug\|path>, or VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT= · exit 1 · < 1 s |
| every ghost error exit 0              | =instance/run bogus= → =unknown target 'bogus' — expected all \| runtime \| kajuit= exit 1 · =registry/tap= bare → exit 1 · duplicate signup → =Client error: 400 — Username already taken= exit 1 |
| =ledger/init= headless crash          | =< /dev/null= → =persisted false=, scaffolds, exit 0, on both distros                                  |
| =instance/auth= verb                  | =instance/lighthouse signup reader secret= → =status SUCCESS= + identity + authority, on both distros  |

* the harness — how a walker walks

Constraints the walkers ran under (all measured in the previous walk, all still true):

- =.ikiro/hooks/vcs-guard.sh= denies any Bash tool string carrying a =git <word>= token that is not a
  read-only verb — =docker exec c "git clone …"= included. Scripts are therefore written with the
  Write tool, =docker cp='d to =/walk/= in the container, and run as =docker exec <c> bash /walk/stepN.sh=.
- =ledger/init= and =instance/init= are Ink/cliffy TUIs: driven by =expect= under =spawn bash -li=,
  prompt regex =# $=, text and =\r= sent separately with ~1 s pacing (Ink drops a chunked Enter).
- =instance/run= never returns → =setsid nohup … &= then a curl poll on =:2501/status= and =:1794/=.
- =pkill -f= matches its own =bash -c= wrapper → bracket trick =ru[n]=.
- =instances/use= is pid-scoped (=~/.viva/sessions/<PPID>.json=) → every script re-selects.
- Placeholder secrets only: =sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key=, JWT from =openssl rand -base64 24=,
  admin =admin= / =changeme=, second user =reader= / =secret=.
- The host repo is read-only reference; nothing in it was edited by either walker.

Per-step deliverable contract (what each report section answers): commands as typed · exit code ·
wall time · full stdout+stderr in =stepN.log= · verdict ∈ =PASS= · =PASS-WITH-DEVIATION= · =FAIL= ·
the README's claim vs what printed.

One walker overstep, logged for the record: the Alpine walker ran =docker builder prune -f --filter
until=24h= on the HOST when the docker VM disk hit 97 % mid-install. Build cache older than a day
only — no image, container, or volume — but it was a host mutation outside the brief. Callout in
=zettelkasten.md=; the next brief carries an explicit "no host-side state change" line.

*** packages I had to add beyond what the README names

The README says: "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites; the repo carries everything else."
On a stock =ubuntu:24.04= the probe before any install printed:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
MISSING curl
MISSING unzip
MISSING git
MISSING ca-certificates
MISSING openssl
MISSING expect
MISSING sqlite3
MISSING deno
ls: cannot access '/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt': No such file or directory
#+END_EXAMPLE

| package         | size    | why                                                                                   |
|-----------------+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| =curl=          | 555 kB  | the README's own line 1 is a =curl= invocation                                        |
| =ca-certificates= | 333 kB | no CA bundle at all; TLS to deno.land / github.com fails without it                   |
| =unzip=         | 491 kB  | =install.sh= unzips =deno.zip=; without it the install aborts                          |
| =git=           | 21.8 MB | README line 3                                                                          |
| =expect=        | harness | the two wizards (=ledger/init=, =instance/init=) are Ink TUIs and need a pty           |
| =openssl=       | harness | to make the JWT the wizard's own hint text tells you to make with =openssl rand -base64 24= |
| =sqlite3=       | harness | to read the identity table                                                             |

Only the first four are prerequisites a reader would hit; the last three are my instrumentation.
=apt-get update= + install of the first four took 21 s.

*** verdict

PASS-WITH-DEVIATION — every README command exits 0 and =viva ledger/doctor= works, but the image needs three unnamed packages first and =viva= is unreachable from any shell other than the one that ran the export.

*** readme delta

- "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites; the repo carries everything else." — false on a stock ubuntu:24.04: =curl=, =ca-certificates= and =unzip= are all missing and all three are needed by the README's own first line. (defect 1)
- "=# open a fresh shell or `$ source ~/.bashrc # ~/.zshrc`=" — the deno 2.9.6 installer writes nothing to any rc file. A fresh shell has neither =deno= nor =viva=, and =source ~/.bashrc= changes nothing. The only thing that works is repeating the =export= line. (defect 2)
- "=deno task install= — dry-runs the system and links =viva= into =~/.deno/bin=" — it links =viva= and writes =~/.config/viva/env=, but nothing is dry-run. =deno.jsonc:29= is =deno task dependencies && deno task ghost/install=; =ghost/install.sh= is 9 lines of =mkdir= / =ln -sf= / =echo=. (defect 3)
- Side effect the README does not mention: this first =viva= invocation already creates =~/.viva= and =~/.viva/registry.json= — before step 1's =ledger/init=. (defect 14)

*** readme delta

The README prints this expected block:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
viva ledger/doctor

scopes
✓ ledger         ~/.viva
✓ repository     ~/vivalence
✓ registry       ~/.viva/registry
✗ instance        — see steps #3-#5
  ✗ mountpoint     —
#+END_EXAMPLE

Deltas, precisely:
- the report's own header line is =viva doctor=, not =viva ledger/doctor=;
- paths are printed absolute (=/root/.viva=, =/root/vivalence=), never tilde-abbreviated;
- =— see steps #3-#5= is README editorial; the tool prints only =—=, and the =instance= row's dash sits one column further left than the README shows;
- the README shows 6 lines; the real report is 53 lines across ten sections (identity, scopes, environment, registry, processes, instances, instance, locks, logs, vip cache). (defect 13)
- "Creates the =ledger=" — =~/.viva= and =~/.viva/registry.json= already existed, created by the first =viva= run in step 0 (10:14 vs =ledger/init= at 10:17). =ledger/init= adds =.env=, =instances/=, =instances.json=, =locks/=, =logs/=, =sessions/= and persists the mount. (defect 14)

*** verdict

PASS-WITH-DEVIATION — the tap works exactly as described (remote clone into the ledger's =/registry=, recorded, 21 modes resolvable), but the README's expected table disagrees with reality on a mode count and on formatting.

*** readme delta

README's expected block:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
packages
mount                            owner        modes  identifier
~/vivalence/registry/fixtures    @fixtures    3      @fixtures/package/fixtures
~/vivalence/registry/playground  @playground  10     @playground/package/playground
~/vivalence/registry/viva        @viva        11     @viva/package/viva
#+END_EXAMPLE

- mode count for =@viva=: README says *11*, reality is *10*. (fixtures 3 ✓, playground 10 ✓.) Confirmed independently by =ledger/doctor=, which enumerates @viva as instance×2 + package×1 + datamap×1 + hallucinator×4 + lighthouse×1 + service×1 = 10, and totals "23 modes 3 packages". (defect 4)
- mounts are printed absolute *and truncated to the terminal width* with a leading =…= (=…oot/vivalence/registry/viva=); the identifier column truncates too (=…ayground/package/playground=). At 80 columns nothing in that table is copy-pasteable. The README shows untruncated =~/=-style paths that the tool never emits. (defect 4)
- the record shape is inconsistent: the three seeded packages are absolute paths, the tapped one is the bare string ="registry-education"=. (defect 5)
- README: "The first =viva= you ran already seeded the record from the checkout's =registry/=" — TRUE, verified: =registry.json= carried all three before =ledger/init= ran.
- the =tap= report's =record= field spills the whole prior record list across three unlabelled lines — cosmetic, but it reads as if =record= had four values.

$ cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env               # unmasked — placeholders
## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="BmP4QlesfqlwdO9rQRtNw8yN6WLLQUNb"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"

*** verdict

PASS-WITH-DEVIATION — the wizard does everything the README promises (seeds =.env=, boots the instance once, signs up the admin, tears down; =auth/signup SUCCESS=, exit 0 in 20 s), but it never returns on its own and the pre-run =lighthouse= failure voice is a raw JS stack.

*** readme delta

- "=instance/init= completes a boot lifecycle. It populates =.env= … It runs the instance once. It asks you to create the first user." — all true, but the README omits that after the signup the wizard *parks* on a "ready / enter → stop + exit" screen and blocks until you press Enter. My first pass sat on that screen for ten minutes. (defect 17)
- "To sign up additional users, use =viva instance/lighthouse signup <username> <password>= — it talks to the running lighthouse, so the instance has to be up (step 5)." — the *documentation* is right, the *voice* is not: with the instance down you get 18 lines of Deno/TypeError stack and a =[probe …] fetch failed= line before the one-line =ghost: Network request failed @ …=. Nothing in the output says "start the instance first". (defect 6)
- "Running =viva instance/doctor= should provide you with an overview of the environment, runtime, clients, daemons, and services." — it does, but at 80 columns the =env= table clips every key to 12 characters, so six =PUBLIC_VIVA_= rows are indistinguishable. (defect 15)
- The README's =viva instances/use hello-world= comment says "pid level bind"; that bind is =VIVA_PROCESS_ID=$PPID= and is silently lost under any forking wrapper (=timeout=, =xargs=). (defect 10)

** step 5 — =viva instance/run=

*** verdict

PASS-WITH-DEVIATION — everything the README promises works (two supervised children, two locks, 404 on =/=, ALIVE on =/status=, HTML on :1794, per-process runs, clean stop), but the bare command the README shows fails in a fresh shell, the run banner is noisier than documented, and an invalid process name is silently accepted through the chain grammar.

*** readme delta

README's expected block:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
viva instance/run

run runtime=8924 kajuit=8925
  VITE v6.3.3  ready in 1467 ms
  ➜  Local:   http://127.0.0.1:1794/
launching on http://localhost:2501/
Status:ALIVE
#+END_EXAMPLE

- *bare =viva instance/run= does not run anything in a fresh shell.* It exits 1 with =ghost: instance: nothing selected …=. The README's §5 block silently assumes you are still in the same shell that ran §4's =instances/use=. Nothing in §5 says so. (defect 2's sibling; see deviations)
- the real banner is 15 lines, not 5: it adds =Task run …=, =Task watch …=, two SvelteKit =config.kit.files.*= deprecation warnings, a =vite-plugin-svelte= "hydratable option has been removed" warning with a =svelte.dev/e/…= URL, and prints =Status:ALIVE= three times in three shapes (=Status:ALIVE= before the VITE banner, =Status:ALIVE= after =launching on=, and =$[runtime:runtime] Status:ALIVE=). Ordering also differs: the README shows the VITE banner first, reality shows a bare =Status:ALIVE= first. (defect 12)
- the process/port table is exactly right: runtime → =http://localhost:2501=, kajuit → =http://localhost:1794=, one lock each, =all= is the default, =viva instance/stop= clears both locks and both ports.
- "The runtime answers =/= with a 404 by design" — TRUE, verified (=HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found=, body =null=).
- =curl http://localhost:2501/status= matches the README's shape byte-for-byte in structure. But "Its pulse is =/status=" oversells it: the timestamp is frozen at boot, identical across calls 4 s (and 53 s) apart. (defect 11)
- "Run one child on its own with =viva instance/run runtime= or =viva instance/run kajuit=" — TRUE, each writes only its own lock. But a *typo'd* child name is a silent no-op through the chained grammar. (defect 9)

*** verdict

PASS-WITH-DEVIATION — five of the six checkboxes verify cleanly; claim 1's second half and claim 3's "from any shell" are false as literally written, because =~/.deno/bin= never reaches =PATH= outside the shell that ran the export.

*** readme delta

- "[x] =deno task install= — =viva= linked into =~/.deno/bin=" — the symlink exists, but the directory is not on =PATH= in any new shell, so the practical claim ("this is done, you can move on") does not hold. (defect 2)
- "[x] =registry/tap= — the =@viva= package recorded; its modes resolve by reference *from any shell*" — the resolution itself is genuinely shell-independent (verified from =cwd=/= with no repo relationship: =@viva/instance/multiplayer= resolved to =registry/viva/instances/starter=), but only from a shell where =viva= is on =PATH=, which is not "any shell". (defect 2)
- claims 2, 4, 5, 6 hold exactly as written.
- Note in passing: the reference =@viva/instance/multiplayer= resolves out of a directory named =starter=, not =multiplayer= — reference identity is record-authoritative, not path-derived. Not a README claim; recorded for completeness.

** timings

| step                                                    | wall time |
|---------------------------------------------------------+-----------|
| 0 · apt prep (NOT in README): update + 4 packages       | 21 s      |
| 0 · =curl … install.sh \| sh=                            | 4 s       |
| 0 · =git clone=                                          | 34 s      |
| 0 · =deno task install=                                  | 19 s      |
| 0 · first bare =viva= (cold jsr fetch)                   | 1 s       |
| 0 · =viva ledger/doctor=                                 | 1 s       |
| 1 · =viva ledger/init= (pty, all defaults)               | < 1 s     |
| 1 · =viva ledger/init= headless                          | 1 s       |
| 1 · =viva ledger/doctor=                                 | 1 s       |
| 2 · =viva registry/list=                                 | 1 s       |
| 2 · =viva registry/tap <github url>=                     | 13 s      |
| 3 · =viva instance/create @viva/instance/hello-world=    | 2 s       |
| 3 · =viva instances/list=                                | < 1 s     |
| 4 · =viva instance/init= (pty wizard, warm caches)       | 20 s      |
| 4 · =viva instance/doctor=                               | < 1 s     |
| 5 · bare =viva instance/run= refusal                     | < 1 s     |
| 5 · detached run → kajuit :1794 first answers            | 4.3 s     |
| 5 · detached run → runtime :2501/status first answers    | 5.4 s     |
| 5 · =viva instance/run runtime= → :2501 up               | 4.1 s     |
| 5 · =viva instance/run kajuit= → :1794 up                | 3.7 s     |
| 5 · =viva instance/stop=                                 | < 1 s     |
| 6 · checklist probes + relaunch, both ports up           | 10 s      |
|---------------------------------------------------------+-----------|
| README path total, excluding apt prep                    | ≈ 1 m 55 s |
| README path total, including apt prep                    | ≈ 2 m 16 s |

The README's headline is "A 'Hello World' in 5 minutes." On warm-ish hardware the machine time fits; the wall-clock cost for a newcomer is dominated by the two interactive wizards and by discovering the missing packages and the PATH problem.

** defects

1. *Undeclared prerequisites.* Symptom, on stock =ubuntu:24.04= before any install:
   ="MISSING curl / MISSING unzip / MISSING ca-certificates"= and
   ="ls: cannot access '/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt': No such file or directory"=.
   Repro: =docker run --rm ubuntu:24.04 sh -c 'command -v curl unzip; ls /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt'=.
   Contradicts README §0: "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites; the repo carries everything else." The README's own line 1 is a =curl= call and =install.sh= shells out to =unzip=.
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer*.

2. *=viva= is unreachable from a fresh shell; the README's stated remedy does not work.* Symptom:
   =$ bash -lc 'command -v viva'= → ="viva NOT on PATH"=; =$ bash -lc 'source ~/.bashrc; command -v viva'= → ="viva STILL NOT on PATH after source ~/.bashrc"=; =grep -i deno ~/.bashrc /etc/profile /root/.profile= → no output.
   Repro: run the five README lines, then open any new shell.
   Contradicts README §0 (="# open a fresh shell or `$ source ~/.bashrc # ~/.zshrc`"=), README §0 tail ("From here on you should be able to run =$ viva="), README §6 claim 1 ("=viva= linked into =~/.deno/bin=") and claim 3 ("its modes resolve by reference from any shell"). Deno 2.9.6's installer no longer edits shell rc files and does not create =~/.deno/env=.
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer*.

3. *=deno task install= does not dry-run anything.* Symptom: its entire terminal output after dependency resolution is
   ="Task ghost/install sh systems/ghost/install.sh \"$(pwd)\""= then ="viva → /root/vivalence  ·  config: /root/.config/viva/env"=.
   Repro: =deno task install= in a fresh clone; read =deno.jsonc:29= and =systems/ghost/install.sh=.
   Contradicts README §0: "=deno task install=  # dry-runs the system and links =viva= into =~/.deno/bin=".
   Severity: *misleads*.

4. *=registry/list= mode count and formatting disagree with the README's table.* Symptom:
   =…oot/vivalence/registry/viva  @viva        10     @viva/package/viva= (README says =11=), and every mount/identifier is width-truncated with a leading =…=.
   Repro: =viva registry/list= in an 80-column terminal.
   Contradicts README §2's expected block (=~/vivalence/registry/viva    @viva    11    @viva/package/viva=) and its untruncated =~/= paths.
   Severity: *misleads*.

5. *Mixed record shape in =registry.json=.* Symptom: =["/root/vivalence/registry/fixtures", "/root/vivalence/registry/playground", "/root/vivalence/registry/viva", "registry-education"]= — three absolute paths and one bare name.
   Repro: =viva registry/tap https://github.com/vivalence/registry-education; cat ~/.viva/registry.json=.
   Contradicts README §2's even-handed description ("Local is recorded into the ledger, remote is cloned into … the ledger's default =/registry=  — Either way, the package is recorded").
   Severity: *misleads*.

6. *=instance/lighthouse= vomits a JS stack when the instance is down.* Symptom, 18 stderr lines beginning
   ="[probe 10:32:38.519] fetch failed http://localhost:2501/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer/auth/signup TypeError: fetch failed"= and ending ="ghost: Network request failed @ /attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer/auth/signup"=, exit 1.
   Repro: =viva instances/use hello-world; viva instance/stop; viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret=.
   Contradicts README §4's own framing ("it talks to the running lighthouse, so the instance has to be up (step 5)") — the tool never says that; the reader gets a crash-looking trace instead.
   Severity: *misleads*.

7. *Piping any report into =head=/a pager crashes ghost with EPIPE.* Symptom:
   ="error: Uncaught Error: write EPIPE"= followed by ~2.5 KB of ink/react-reconciler frames; exit 1, 2567 bytes on stderr. The same command redirected to a file exits 0 with 0 bytes of stderr.
   Repro: =viva instance/doctor | head -2=.
   Contradicts nothing in the README textually, but the README teaches =viva … doctor= as the inspection verb, and the first thing anyone does with a 53-line report is pipe it.
   Severity: *misleads* (blocks scripting).

8. *The =--instance=<path>= flag ghost advertises is rejected in front of the verb.* Symptom:
   =$ viva --instance=hello-world instance/doctor= → ="ghost: no handler for --instance=hello-world instance/doctor / try: viva help"=, exit 127. After the verb it works (exit 0).
   Repro: as above; compare with bare =viva='s own footer =flags  --json  --buffer  --help  --env=<path>  --ledger=<path>  --repository=<path>  --registry=<path>  --instance=<path>  --mountpoint=<path>= and with the "nothing selected" error, which recommends =--instance=<slug|path>=.
   Severity: *misleads*.

9. *The chained grammar silently swallows an invalid process target.* Symptom:
   =$ viva instance/run bogus= → ="ghost: instance: unknown target 'bogus' — expected all | runtime | kajuit"=, exit 1 (correct);
   =$ viva instances/use hello-world run bogus= → no stdout, no stderr, *exit 0*; same for =… stop bogus=. A bogus *verb* is still caught (=exit 127=).
   Repro: both lines above, back to back.
   Contradicts README §5 ("Run one child on its own with =viva instance/run runtime= or =viva instance/run kajuit=") read together with §4's chain grammar ("or as one: =viva instances/use hello-world init=").
   Severity: *misleads*.

10. *The pid-level bind is lost under any forking wrapper, and session records are never reaped.* Symptom, in one script, immediately after a successful =viva instances/use hello-world=:
    =$ timeout 60 viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret= → ="ghost: instance: nothing selected — viva instances/use <slug>, --instance=<slug|path>, or VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT"=, while the un-wrapped call in the same script reaches the network. =env= and =nohup= are fine (they =exec=); =timeout= forks.
    Repro: the two lines above; cause is =systems/ghost/ghost.sh=: =VIVA_PROCESS_ID="${VIVA_PROCESS_ID:-$PPID}"=.
    Related: =~/.viva/sessions/= accumulated 5 stale =<pid>.json= files during one walk and nothing removes them; a recycled pid inherits a dead selection.
    Contradicts README §4's "=# pid level bind=" being presented as a simple, reliable selection.
    Severity: *misleads*.

11. *=/status='s timestamp is frozen at boot, not a pulse.* Symptom: two calls 4 s apart, and a third 53 s later, all returned
    ={"timestamp":"2026-09-04T10:36:30.957Z","code":"ALIVE","label":"http://localhost:2501/"}= while =date -u= said =2026-09-04T10:37:23.561Z=.
    Repro: =curl -s :2501/status; sleep 4; curl -s :2501/status; date -u=.
    Contradicts README §5: "Its pulse is =/status=" — a frozen timestamp cannot distinguish a live runtime from a stale one.
    Severity: *misleads*.

12. *The run banner in the README is a cleaned-up idealisation.* Symptom, the real first 15 lines add =Task run …=, =Task watch …=, ="The `config.kit.files.routes` option is deprecated, and will be removed in a future version"=, the same for =appTemplate=, and ="[vite-plugin-svelte] … The `hydratable` option has been removed. Svelte components are always hydratable now / https://svelte.dev/e/options_removed_hydratable"=; =Status:ALIVE= appears three times in three shapes, and the first one precedes the VITE banner (README shows VITE first).
    Repro: =viva instances/use hello-world run= and read the first 15 lines.
    Contradicts README §5's 5-line expected block.
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

13. *README §1's doctor block does not match the tool's output.* Symptom: header is =viva doctor= not =viva ledger/doctor=; paths are =/root/.viva= and =/root/vivalence= not =~/.viva= and =~/vivalence=; =— see steps #3-#5= is never printed; the real report is 53 lines / 10 sections against the README's 6.
    Repro: =viva ledger/doctor=.
    Contradicts README §1's "The doctor should tell you that:" block.
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

14. *The ledger is created before =ledger/init=.* Symptom: =~/.viva= and =~/.viva/registry.json= are timestamped 10:14, created by the first bare =viva= / =viva ledger/doctor= in step 0; =ledger/init= ran at 10:17.
    Repro: run the five README lines, then =ls -la ~/.viva= before touching =ledger/init=.
    Contradicts README §1: "Creates the =ledger= — the one directory Vivalence owns on your machine".
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

15. *=instance/doctor='s env table clips keys to 12 characters.* Symptom: six consecutive rows read =PUBLIC_VIVA_= with no way to tell them apart; the =at= column is clipped mid-word (=clients.kajui=, =daemon[playgr=, =lighthouse.st=).
    Repro: =viva instance/doctor= at 80 columns.
    Contradicts README §4: "should provide you with an overview of the environment …".
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

16. *=instances/list= changes shape with row count, and there is no verb to drop a record.* Symptom: with one instance it prints an indented block headed by an index =0=; with two it prints a =slug | running | mount= table. =viva help instances= lists only =use=, =tap=, =rename=, =list= — no untap, though =registry/untap= exists — so an instance whose directory you delete stays in =instances.json= with a mount that no longer exists.
    Repro: =viva instance/create @viva/instance/multiplayer /tmp/x; rm -rf /tmp/x; viva instances/list=.
    Contradicts nothing in the README; recorded because I tripped it and had to hand-edit =instances.json=.
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

17. *=instance/init= never returns on its own.* Symptom: after ="● auth/signup SUCCESS 01a06bf8-…"= the TUI parks on a frame reading ="next steps / 1. enter to stop + exit"= and ="enter → stop + exit"= and blocks. My first pass sat there ten minutes with the instance up and both ports serving.
    Repro: =viva instances/use hello-world; viva instance/init= and do not press Enter.
    Contradicts README §4's three-sentence description ("It populates =.env= … It runs the instance once. It asks you to create the first user."), which reads as a command that finishes.
    Severity: *cosmetic* (a reader in a terminal simply presses Enter; it blocks only automation).

** deviations from README

Every command I ran that the README does not show, and why.

1. =apt-get update && apt-get install -y curl ca-certificates unzip git= — the stock image has none of them. The README's own line 1 cannot execute without =curl= + a CA bundle, and =install.sh= cannot unpack =deno.zip= without =unzip=. Recorded as defect 1.
2. =apt-get install -y expect openssl= — instrumentation. Both wizards (=ledger/init=, =instance/init=) are Ink TUIs and need a pty; =expect= supplies one and records a typescript. =openssl= generates the JWT the wizard's own hint text asks for (="Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24"=).
3. =apt-get install -y sqlite3= — instrumentation, to read the identity table.

4. =setsid nohup bash -lc '… run' &= plus a =curl= poll loop — =instance/run= is a foreground supervisor that never returns (=help= calls it "run the mounted instance attached (foreground)"), so it must be detached to probe the ports from the same session.
5. =viva instances/use hello-world run= instead of bare =viva instance/run= for every detached start — a detached shell has a different pid and therefore no session bind. This is the README's own chain grammar (§4: "or as one: =viva instances/use hello-world init="), applied to =run=.

6. =viva instance/stop=, =viva instances/list=, =viva instance/doctor=, =viva help …=, and =ls= / =cat= / =find= / =ps= / =curl= / =sqlite3= probes throughout — inspection only, no state change except the stops.
7. *Second pass of step 4.* My first =instance/init= run completed (=.env= written, =admin= created, =auth/signup SUCCESS=) but my expect matcher missed the "enter → stop + exit" park screen, so I had to kill the exec without an exit code. I then removed =~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env=, its =mountpoint/=, and the stale locks and logs, and re-ran =viva instance/init= from scratch to capture a clean exit code (=RC:0=, 20 s). Both transcripts are kept (=step4.log= / =step4.typescript= = first pass, =step4b.log= / =step4b.typescript= = second).
8. =viva instance/create @viva/instance/multiplayer /tmp/resolve-probe= (from a shell with =cwd=/=) — to test README §6 claim 3's "resolve by reference from any shell" with a reference other than the one already on the shelf. I then =rm -rf /tmp/resolve-probe= and, because no verb removes an instance record, hand-edited =~/.viva/instances.json= to drop the leftover =multiplayer= key. See defect 16.
9. Isolation probes added after anomalies surfaced: =viva instance/lighthouse login reader secret=; a duplicate =signup= (→ ="ghost: Client error: 400 — Username already taken"=, exit 1); =--instance== before vs after the verb; =viva instance/doctor | head -2= vs redirected; =timeout= / =env= / =nohup= wrappers around a bound =viva=; =viva instance/run bogus= direct vs chained; two =/status= calls 4 s apart. These produced defects 6–11.
10. =git log -1 --format='%H %ci %an %s'= and =sha256sum README.md= inside the clone — read-only, to pin the HEAD and prove the README under test is the ground-truth text.
11. =viva registry/list= / =ledger/doctor= run twice (before and after a step) where the README shows them once — to establish before/after deltas.

** leave-behind state

The container =readmen-ubuntu= is left *running*, with the instance up:

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
:2501 -> 404      (runtime, 404 on / by design)
:1794 -> 200      (kajuit, HTML)
/status -> {"timestamp":"2026-09-04T10:36:30.957Z","code":"ALIVE","label":"http://localhost:2501/"}
locks: hello-world_kajuit.lock  hello-world_runtime.lock
identities: admin, reader
no host ports published
#+END_EXAMPLE

* walk — ubuntu — scripts (as run)

** step0a.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# NOT IN README. Stock ubuntu:24.04 prep. Record exactly what the image lacks, then add the minimum.
echo "===== A. what the stock image is ====="
cat /etc/os-release
uname -m
echo
echo "===== B. what the stock image LACKS (README claims 'Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites') ====="
for t in curl unzip git ca-certificates openssl expect sqlite3 deno; do
  p=$(command -v "$t" 2>/dev/null)
  if [ -n "$p" ]; then echo "PRESENT $t -> $p"; else echo "MISSING $t"; fi
done
echo "--- ca-certificates bundle:"
ls -la /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt 2>&1
echo
echo "===== C. system prep (unstated by README) ====="
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
set -x
time apt-get update -qq
time apt-get install -y -qq curl ca-certificates unzip git
set +x
echo "--- exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== D. after prep ====="
for t in curl unzip git ca-certificates; do
  p=$(command -v "$t" 2>/dev/null); echo "$t -> ${p:-<no binary, package only>}"
done
git --version
curl --version | head -1
unzip -v | head -1
echo "===== E. dpkg sizes added ====="
dpkg-query -W -f='${Package} ${Version} ${Installed-Size}kB\n' curl ca-certificates unzip git 2>&1
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §0 — Install. Lines 1-4 of the README's five-line block, VERBATIM, in a fresh non-interactive shell.
cd ~
echo "===== README line 1: curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== is deno on PATH in THIS shell (before the export)? ====="
command -v deno && echo "yes" || echo "no - deno NOT on PATH in this shell"
echo
echo "===== README line 2: export PATH=\"\$HOME/.deno/bin:\$PATH\" ====="
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
command -v deno; deno --version
echo "--- README badge claims deno 2.7+"
echo
echo "===== what the installer wrote ====="
ls -la ~/.deno/bin ~/.deno/env* 2>&1
echo "--- ~/.bashrc tail 10:"; tail -10 ~/.bashrc
echo "--- ~/.bash_profile:"; ls -la ~/.bash_profile 2>&1; cat ~/.bash_profile 2>&1
echo
echo "===== README line 3: vcs clone https://github.com/vivalence/vivalence.git ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
git clone https://github.com/vivalence/vivalence.git
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== README line 4: cd vivalence ====="
cd vivalence && pwd
echo "--- HEAD (read-only):"
git log -1 --format='%H %ci %an %s'
git status --short | head -20
echo "--- checkout size:"; du -sh ~/vivalence
echo "--- README checksum in the clone:"
sha256sum README.md
wc -l README.md
#+END_SRC

export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~/vivalence || exit 9

echo "(no output above = nothing added; the export in README line 2 is the only thing putting deno on PATH)"

#!/bin/bash
# README §0 tail: "From here on you should be able to run `$ viva`. Test this with f.E. `$ viva ledger/doctor`."
echo "===== what install wrote ====="
echo "--- ~/.deno/bin:"; ls -la ~/.deno/bin
echo "--- cat ~/.config/viva/env:"; cat ~/.config/viva/env; echo "(exit=$?)"
echo "--- ~/.config/viva:"; ls -la ~/.config/viva
echo
echo "===== A. FRESH LOGIN SHELL (bash -lc), exactly what a reader gets in a new terminal ====="
bash -lc 'echo "PATH=$PATH"; command -v viva || echo "viva NOT on PATH"; command -v deno || echo "deno NOT on PATH"'
echo "--- exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== B. fresh INTERACTIVE login shell (bash -lic) ====="
bash -lic 'command -v viva || echo "viva NOT on PATH"' 2>&1
echo
echo "===== C. README's own remedy: source ~/.bashrc ====="
bash -lc 'source ~/.bashrc 2>/dev/null; command -v viva || echo "viva STILL NOT on PATH after source ~/.bashrc"'
echo
echo "===== D. with the README line-2 export applied ====="
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
command -v viva; ls -la $(command -v viva)
echo
echo "===== E. bare \`viva\` ====="
t0=$(date +%s); viva; echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== F. viva ledger/doctor (README's suggested smoke test, BEFORE ledger/init) ====="
t0=$(date +%s); viva ledger/doctor; echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"

** step1.exp
#+BEGIN_SRC expect
#!/usr/bin/expect -f
# README §1 under a pty: `viva ledger/init`, accepting every default.
set timeout 120
log_file -noappend /walk/step1.typescript
spawn bash -li
expect -re {# $}
send "export PATH=\"\$HOME/.deno/bin:\$PATH\"\r"
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP1-BEGIN; viva ledger/init; echo RC:\$?\r"
expect -re {STEP1-BEGIN\r}

# prompt 1: "How is vivalence(viva) embedded?"  (select: .config / manual / abort) -> default
expect {
  -re {embedded} { sleep 1.5; send "\r"; sleep 1.5 }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> ledger/init exited before prompt 1" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for prompt 1" }
}
# prompt 2: "where does $viva live? $VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT" (input, default /root/.viva) -> default
expect {
  -re {VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT} { sleep 1.5; send "\r"; sleep 1.5 }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> no mount prompt" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for prompt 2" }
}
expect {
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { }
  -re {›} { sleep 1; send "\r"; exp_continue }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for RC"; send "\r"; expect -re {RC:[0-9]} }
}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP1-DOCTOR; viva ledger/doctor; echo RC:\$?\r"
expect -re {STEP1-DOCTOR\r}
expect -re {RC:[0-9]}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP1-TREE; ls -la ~/.viva; echo ---; cat ~/.config/viva/env; echo RC:\$?\r"
expect -re {RC:[0-9]}
expect -re {# $}
send "exit\r"
expect eof
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §1, non-tty probes.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
echo "===== A. ledger contents after ledger/init ====="
ls -la ~/.viva
echo "--- cat ~/.viva/.env:"; cat ~/.viva/.env
echo "--- cat ~/.viva/instances.json:"; cat ~/.viva/instances.json; echo
echo "--- cat ~/.viva/registry.json:"; cat ~/.viva/registry.json; echo
echo "--- cat ~/.config/viva/env:"; cat ~/.config/viva/env
echo
echo "===== B. viva ledger/doctor WITHOUT a tty — how many times does it print? ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
viva ledger/doctor > /tmp/doc.txt 2>&1; echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo "--- occurrences of the report header 'viva doctor': $(grep -c '^viva doctor' /tmp/doc.txt)"
echo "--- scopes block as printed:"
sed -n '/^scopes/,/mountpoint/p' /tmp/doc.txt
echo "--- full line count: $(wc -l < /tmp/doc.txt)"
echo
echo "===== C. viva ledger/init WITHOUT a tty (headless path) ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
timeout 60 viva ledger/init < /dev/null 2>&1 | head -40
echo "--- exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]} wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== D. ledger still intact after the headless attempt? ====="
ls ~/.viva
cat ~/.config/viva/env

#!/usr/bin/expect -f
# README §4 under a pty:  viva instances/use hello-world  ->  viva instance/init
# form 1: SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY + SECRET_VIVA_JWT ; form 2: admin username/password
set timeout 900
log_file -noappend /walk/step4.typescript
set jwt [exec openssl rand -base64 24]
proc type {text} { send -- $text; sleep 0.8; send "\r"; sleep 1.2 }
spawn bash -li
expect -re {# $}
send "export PATH=\"\$HOME/.deno/bin:\$PATH\"; cd ~\r"
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-USE; viva instances/use hello-world; echo RC:\$?\r"
expect -re {STEP4-USE\r}
expect -re {RC:[0-9]}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-INIT; date +%s; viva instance/init; echo RC:\$?; date +%s\r"
expect -re {STEP4-INIT\r}

expect {
  -re {ANTHROPIC_API_KEY} { sleep 2 }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the secrets form" ; exit }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for the secrets form" }
}
type "sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
type $jwt
sleep 0.5
send "\r"

expect {
  -re {username} { sleep 2; type "admin"; type "changeme"; sleep 0.5; send "\r" }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the admin form" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for the admin form" }
}
expect {
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { }
  -re {enter to continue|press enter} { sleep 1; send "\r"; exp_continue }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for init RC; sending enter"; send "\r"; expect -re {RC:[0-9]} }
}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-DONE; exit\r"
expect eof

# README §4 under a pty, second pass on a WIPED instance so the exit code is captured.
#   viva instances/use hello-world  ->  viva instance/init
# form 1: SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY + SECRET_VIVA_JWT ; form 2: admin username/password
# the wizard parks on "enter -> stop + exit" and needs one more Enter to return.

log_file -noappend /walk/step4b.typescript
set jwt [exec openssl rand -base64 24]
proc type {text} { send -- $text; sleep 0.8; send "\r"; sleep 1.2 }

expect -re {# $}

expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-USE; viva instances/use hello-world; echo RC:\$?\r"

expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-INIT; T0=\$(date +%s); viva instance/init; echo RC:\$?; echo WALL:\$((\$(date +%s)-T0))s\r"

expect {
  -re {ANTHROPIC_API_KEY} { sleep 2 }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the secrets form" ; exit }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for the secrets form" }
}
type "sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
type $jwt
sleep 0.5
send "\r"

expect {
  -re {username} { sleep 2; type "admin"; type "changeme"; sleep 0.5; send "\r" }
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the admin form" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for the admin form" }
}
# the wizard boots the instance, signs up, then parks on a "ready / enter -> stop + exit" screen
expect {
  -re {RC:[0-9]} { }
  -re {stop \+ exit} { puts "\n>>> reached the park screen; sending Enter"; sleep 2; send "\r"; exp_continue }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> timeout waiting for init RC; sending Enter"; send "\r"; exp_continue }
}
expect -re {WALL:}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-ENV; cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env; echo RC:\$?\r"
expect -re {RC:[0-9]}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-DONE; exit\r"

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §4 tail — with the instance NOT running.
# Every `viva` in this script shares this script's pid as VIVA_PROCESS_ID (ghost.sh uses $PPID),
# so one `instances/use` at the top binds the whole script, exactly as the README's shell would.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
echo "===== A. is anything running? ====="
ls -la ~/.viva/locks; for p in 2501 1794; do echo "  :$p -> $(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:$p/)"; done
echo
echo "===== A2. viva instances/use hello-world  (README §4 line 1), non-tty ====="
viva instances/use hello-world; echo "--- exit=$?"
echo "--- session record: $(ls ~/.viva/sessions)"; cat ~/.viva/sessions/*.json 2>/dev/null; echo
echo
echo "===== B. cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env  (placeholders, unmasked) ====="
cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env
echo "--- exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== C. tree of the instance after init ====="
find ~/.viva/instances/hello-world -maxdepth 3 | head -40
echo "  (total entries: $(find ~/.viva/instances/hello-world | wc -l))"
echo
echo "===== D. README: 'viva instance/lighthouse signup <username> <password>' WITH THE INSTANCE DOWN ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
timeout 120 viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== E. viva instance/doctor ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
viva instance/doctor
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
#+END_SRC

** step4d.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# isolate the instance/lighthouse failure voice: is "nothing selected" true, or is it a mis-voiced "instance is down"?
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
echo "===== 1. bind by session, then lighthouse (same shell pid, instance DOWN) ====="
viva instances/use hello-world >/dev/null; echo "  bind exit=$?"
viva instance/doctor | head -2; echo "  doctor exit=$? (doctor CAN see the selection)"
viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret; echo "  lighthouse exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== 2. bind by --instance flag (instance DOWN) ====="
viva --instance=hello-world instance/lighthouse signup reader secret; echo "  exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== 3. bind by VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT env (instance DOWN) ====="
VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT=/root/.viva/instances/hello-world viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret; echo "  exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== 4. same three for instance/doctor, as control ====="
viva --instance=hello-world instance/doctor | head -2; echo "  exit=$?"
VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT=/root/.viva/instances/hello-world viva instance/doctor | head -2; echo "  exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== 5. what does the help say instance/lighthouse takes? ====="
viva help instance/lighthouse
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# follow-ups on the two anomalies seen in step4c/step4d
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
viva instances/use hello-world >/dev/null
echo "===== 1. does a WRAPPER process break the session bind? (ghost.sh sets VIVA_PROCESS_ID=\$PPID) ====="
echo "-- direct:"
viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret 2>&1 | tail -2; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo "-- wrapped in \`timeout\`:"
timeout 60 viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret 2>&1 | tail -2; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo "-- wrapped in \`env\`:"
env viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret 2>&1 | tail -2; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo "-- wrapped in \`nohup\`:"
nohup viva instance/doctor 2>&1 | head -2; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo
echo "===== 2. the --instance flag the \`viva\` help footer advertises ====="
echo "-- before the verb:"; viva --instance=hello-world instance/doctor 2>&1 | head -3; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo "-- after the verb:";  viva instance/doctor --instance=hello-world 2>&1 | head -3; echo "   rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo "-- footer as printed by bare \`viva\`:"; viva 2>/dev/null | grep -A2 '^flags'
echo
echo "===== 3. EPIPE: piping a report into head ====="
viva instance/doctor > /tmp/full-doctor.txt 2>/tmp/full-doctor.err; echo "   redirect rc=$? stderr bytes=$(wc -c < /tmp/full-doctor.err)"
viva instance/doctor 2>/tmp/pipe.err | head -2 >/dev/null; echo "   piped rc=${PIPESTATUS[0]} stderr bytes=$(wc -c < /tmp/pipe.err)"
echo "   first stderr line when piped: $(head -2 /tmp/pipe.err | tail -1)"
echo
echo "===== 4. instance/lighthouse voice with the instance DOWN, stderr and stdout separated ====="
viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret >/tmp/lh.out 2>/tmp/lh.err; echo "   rc=$?"
echo "   stdout ($(wc -l < /tmp/lh.out) lines):"; cat /tmp/lh.out
echo "   stderr ($(wc -l < /tmp/lh.err) lines), first 3 + last 1:"; head -3 /tmp/lh.err; echo "   ..."; tail -1 /tmp/lh.err
#+END_SRC

** step5a.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §5, first form: bare `viva instance/run` in a FRESH shell (new pid, no instances/use bind).
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
echo "my shell pid=$$   (VIVA_PROCESS_ID ghost will use)"
echo "session records on the ledger: $(ls ~/.viva/sessions | tr '\n' ' ')"
echo "is there a record for MY pid? $(ls ~/.viva/sessions/$$.json 2>&1)"
echo
echo "===== README verbatim: viva instance/run ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
viva instance/run
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== stdout/stderr split ====="
viva instance/run >/tmp/r.out 2>/tmp/r.err; echo "rc=$?"
echo "stdout ($(wc -l < /tmp/r.out) lines): $(cat /tmp/r.out)"
echo "stderr ($(wc -l < /tmp/r.err) lines): $(cat /tmp/r.err)"
#+END_SRC

** step5b.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §5 — the run itself. `instances/use <slug> run` is the README §4 chain grammar
# (README line 109: "or as one: viva instances/use hello-world init"), used here because a
# detached shell cannot inherit a pid-level bind.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
echo "=== T0 $(date -u +%H:%M:%S.%3N) : setsid nohup bash -lc 'viva instances/use hello-world run' ==="
setsid nohup bash -lc 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; cd ~; viva instances/use hello-world run' > /walk/step5-run.out 2>&1 &
echo "spawned wrapper pid=$!"
T0=$(date +%s%3N)
RT=""; KJ=""
for i in $(seq 1 150); do
  sleep 1
  R=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:2501/status 2>/dev/null)
  K=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:1794/ 2>/dev/null)
  [ -z "$RT" ] && [ "$R" != "000" ] && { RT=$(( $(date +%s%3N) - T0 )); echo "  runtime :2501/status first answered $R after ${RT}ms"; }
  [ -z "$KJ" ] && [ "$K" != "000" ] && { KJ=$(( $(date +%s%3N) - T0 )); echo "  kajuit  :1794/ first answered $K after ${KJ}ms"; }
  [ -n "$RT" ] && [ -n "$KJ" ] && break
done
echo "  (poll ended at t=$(( ($(date +%s%3N) - T0) / 1000 ))s)"
echo
echo "===== head of the run log (README shows 5 lines here) ====="
head -25 /walk/step5-run.out
echo "  --- total run-log lines so far: $(wc -l < /walk/step5-run.out)"
echo
echo "===== ls ~/.viva/locks with contents ====="
ls -la ~/.viva/locks
for f in ~/.viva/locks/*; do echo "--- $f:"; cat "$f"; echo; done
echo
echo "===== curl -s http://localhost:2501/status ====="
curl -s http://localhost:2501/status; echo; echo "  exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== curl -s -i http://localhost:2501/   (README: 'answers / with a 404 by design') ====="
curl -s -i http://localhost:2501/ | head -12
echo
echo "===== curl -s -i http://localhost:1794/ ====="
curl -s -i http://localhost:1794/ | head -20
echo
echo "===== processes ====="
ps -eo pid,ppid,etime,cmd | grep -E "ghost/mod.js|run.js|vite|kajuit|runtime" | grep -v grep
echo
echo "===== viva instances/list while running ====="
viva instances/list
#+END_SRC

** step5c.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §4/§5: `viva instance/lighthouse signup <user> <pass>` against the RUNNING lighthouse,
# then read the identity table straight out of the instance's sqlite.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
viva instances/use hello-world > /dev/null
echo "===== A. instance up? ====="
for p in 2501 1794; do echo "  :$p -> $(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:$p/)"; done
echo
echo "===== B. README: viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret
echo "--- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "===== C. and login with it ====="
viva instance/lighthouse login reader secret
echo "--- exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== D. a duplicate signup (same username again) ====="
viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret 2>&1 | tail -3
echo "--- exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
echo
echo "===== E. find the sqlite db under the instance mount ====="
find ~/.viva/instances/hello-world -name '*.db'
echo
echo "===== F. install sqlite3 (NOT in the README — needed to read the identity table) ====="
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
apt-get install -y -qq sqlite3 >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "  sqlite3 -> $(command -v sqlite3)  $(sqlite3 --version)"
echo
DB=/root/.viva/instances/hello-world/mountpoint/service_multiplayer/lighthouse.viva.db
echo "===== G. .tables on $DB ====="
sqlite3 "$DB" ".tables"
echo "--- .schema:"
sqlite3 "$DB" ".schema"
echo
echo "===== H. the identity table ====="
sqlite3 -header -column "$DB" "SELECT * FROM user;" 2>&1
echo "--- exit=$?"
echo
echo "===== I. tokens.json ====="
cat /root/.viva/instances/hello-world/mountpoint/service_multiplayer/tokens.json 2>&1 | head -20
#+END_SRC

** step5d.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §5 tail: `viva instance/stop`, then the per-process forms `run runtime|kajuit|bogus`.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
cd ~
viva instances/use hello-world > /dev/null

probe() { echo "    :2501=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:2501/)  :1794=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:1794/)  locks=[$(ls ~/.viva/locks 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' ')]"; }
waitport() { local p=$1; local n=${2:-90}; local t0=$(date +%s%3N); for i in $(seq 1 $n); do sleep 1; [ "$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:$p/)" != "000" ] && { echo "    :$p up after $(( $(date +%s%3N) - t0 ))ms"; return 0; }; done; echo "    :$p NOT up after ${n}s"; return 1; }
stopit() { echo "  --- viva instance/stop"; viva instance/stop 2>&1 | tail -6; echo "      exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"; sleep 3; pkill -f "ghost/mod.js instance/ru[n]" 2>/dev/null; pkill -f "ghost/mod.js instances/use hello-world ru[n]" 2>/dev/null; sleep 2; probe; }

echo "===== A. state before stop ====="; probe
echo "===== B. viva instance/stop ====="
t0=$(date +%s); viva instance/stop; echo "  --- exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
sleep 3
echo "  after stop:"; probe
echo "  leftover processes: $(ps -eo cmd | grep -cE 'ghost/mod.js|run.js|vite@6' )"
ps -eo pid,etime,cmd | grep -E "ghost/mod.js|run.js|vite@6" | grep -v grep
echo "  instances/list:"; viva instances/list
echo
echo "===== C. viva instance/run runtime ====="
setsid nohup bash -lc 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; cd ~; viva instances/use hello-world run runtime' > /walk/step5d-runtime.out 2>&1 &
waitport 2501 60; probe; echo "  head of log:"; head -6 /walk/step5d-runtime.out
stopit
echo
echo "===== D. viva instance/run kajuit ====="
setsid nohup bash -lc 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; cd ~; viva instances/use hello-world run kajuit' > /walk/step5d-kajuit.out 2>&1 &
waitport 1794 60; probe; echo "  head of log:"; head -8 /walk/step5d-kajuit.out
stopit
echo
echo "===== E. viva instance/run bogus ====="
t0=$(date +%s)
timeout 60 bash -lc 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; cd ~; viva instances/use hello-world run bogus' 2>&1 | tail -8
echo "  --- exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]} wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
probe
stopit
#+END_SRC

** step6.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §6 — Summary and next steps. One probe per checkbox claim.
cd ~
pass() { echo "  VERDICT: $1"; }

echo "===== [x] 1. \`deno task install\` — \`viva\` linked into \`~/.deno/bin\`, this checkout pinned in \`~/.config/viva/env\` ====="
echo "  probe: ls -l ~/.deno/bin/viva ; cat ~/.config/viva/env"
ls -l ~/.deno/bin/viva
cat ~/.config/viva/env
echo "  probe: is ~/.deno/bin on PATH in a fresh login shell?"
bash -lc 'case ":$PATH:" in *":$HOME/.deno/bin:"*) echo "    yes";; *) echo "    NO — ~/.deno/bin is not on PATH; \`viva\` is unreachable without the README line-2 export";; esac'
echo
echo "===== [x] 2. \`ledger/init\` — \`~/.viva\` exists ====="
echo "  probe: ls -d ~/.viva && ls ~/.viva"
ls -d ~/.viva && ls ~/.viva
echo
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
echo "===== [x] 3. \`registry/tap\` — the \`@viva\` package recorded; its modes resolve by reference from any shell ====="
echo "  probe: viva registry/list | grep @viva  (from a NEW shell with no bind)"
bash -c 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; viva registry/list' | grep -E '@viva|@education'
echo "  probe: does a reference resolve from a shell that never touched the repo? (cd / first)"
bash -c 'cd /; export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; viva instance/create @viva/instance/multiplayer /tmp/resolve-probe 2>&1 | head -4; echo "    exit=$?"'
rm -rf /tmp/resolve-probe
echo
echo "===== [x] 4. \`instance/create\` — \`hello-world\` copied onto the shelf ====="
echo "  probe: grep hello-world ~/.viva/instances.json ; ls ~/.viva/instances/hello-world"
grep -o '"hello-world"' ~/.viva/instances.json
ls ~/.viva/instances/hello-world
echo
echo "===== [x] 5. \`instance/init\` — the instance's \`.env\` populated, your account registered against its lighthouse ====="
echo "  probe: the two SECRET_ keys are non-empty:"
grep -E '^SECRET_' ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env
echo "  probe: identity table:"
sqlite3 -header -column /root/.viva/instances/hello-world/mountpoint/service_multiplayer/lighthouse.viva.db "SELECT slug, created_at FROM Identity ORDER BY created_at;"
echo
echo "===== [x] 6. \`instance/run\` — runtime serving on :2501, kajuit on :1794 ====="
echo "  starting it for the final probe, detached, and leaving it up:"
setsid nohup bash -lc 'export PATH=$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH; cd ~; viva instances/use hello-world run' > /walk/step6-run.out 2>&1 &
for i in $(seq 1 90); do sleep 1; R=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:2501/status); K=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 2 http://localhost:1794/); [ "$R" = "200" ] && [ "$K" = "200" ] && { echo "    both up after ${i}s"; break; }; done
echo "  probe: curl -s http://localhost:2501/status"
curl -s http://localhost:2501/status; echo
echo "  probe: curl -s http://localhost:1794/ | head -3"
curl -s http://localhost:1794/ | head -3
echo "  probe: locks"; ls ~/.viva/locks
echo
echo "===== final state — the container is LEFT with the instance running ====="
viva instances/use hello-world >/dev/null; viva instances/list
ps -eo pid,etime,cmd | grep -E "ghost/mod.js|run.js|vite@6" | grep -v grep
#+END_SRC

===== E. bare `viva` =====
viva

ledger
  ledger/init              [path]               machine first-run — pick +
persist the ledger home, scaffold locks/logs/registry/instances
  ledger/doctor                                 machine report card — homes,
record, store census, locks, processes, logs

registry
  registry/list                                 every tapped package — mount,
the owner it declares, how many modes it carries, and its identifier
  registry/tap             [source] [target]    tap a package — record a
reference; a remote source clones into the store (or target)
  registry/untap           [reference]          untap a package — record removal
 only, the store keeps the working copy
  registry/bootstrap       [destination] [source] bootstrap a package — a bare
manifest, or a clone of another package renamed to the destination; the result
is tapped

instances
  instances/use            [reference]          select this shell's instance
from the set (VIVA_PROCESS_ID session) — a bare slug resolves against the
ledger, an ambiguous or missing one opens the picker; --ledger writes the
machine default; bare use in a pipe prints current + provenance; trailing
segments chain under /instance (instances/use italian run)
  instances/tap            [path]               adopt a hand-placed instance dir
 into the record — the record is the identity authority; --slug=<slug> required,
 the path is a PATH (./name, never a bare slug)
  instances/rename         [prior] [next]       move an instance's record key —
carries dead locks and the log dir; refuses while running
  instances/list                                every instance on the shelf —
slug, mount, valence, and which are running

instance
  instance/create          [source] [target] [--use] create an instance from a
source — a bare slug resolves against the registry, an ambiguous or missing one
opens the picker; no target lands it in <ledger>/instances/<slug>
  instance/init            [username] [password] instance first-run — seed .env;
 then the wizard (or <user> <pass>) boots, signs up the admin, tears down
  instance/run             [process]            run the mounted instance
attached (foreground)
  instance/start           [process]            start the mounted instance
detached (background)
  instance/stop            [process]            stop the mounted instance
  instance/lighthouse      [action] [username] [password] signup or login
against the mounted instance's lighthouse
  instance/doctor          [target] [filter]    instance report card — manifest,
 services, locks

help
  help                     [filter]             every nature with its params and
 valence; a prefix narrows, an exact nature details

flags  --json  --buffer  --help  --env=<path>  --ledger=<path>
--repository=<path>  --registry=<path>  --instance=<path>  --mountpoint=<path>
viva help <prefix> narrows; an exact nature shows its params

--- cat ~/.viva/.env:
## homes
# Absolute path to the vivalence checkout. Repo-relative resolution needs it.
VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT=""

## keys
# Machine-wide Anthropic key.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=""
# Machine-wide OpenRouter key.
SECRET_VIVA_OPENROUTER_API_KEY=""
# Machine-wide ElevenLabs key.
SECRET_VIVA_ELEVENLABS_API_KEY=""
# Machine-wide Deepgram key.
SECRET_VIVA_DEEPGRAM_API_KEY=""
--- cat ~/.viva/instances.json:
{}
--- cat ~/.viva/registry.json:
[
  "/root/vivalence/registry/fixtures",
  "/root/vivalence/registry/playground",
  "/root/vivalence/registry/viva"
]
--- cat ~/.config/viva/env:
export VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT="/root/vivalence"
export VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT="/root/.viva"

root@988e22bafc70:~#
root@988e22bafc70:~#

root@988e22bafc70:~# < cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env; echo RC:$?
STEP4-ENV
## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="BmP4QlesfqlwdO9rQRtNw8yN6WLLQUNb"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
RC:0
root@988e22bafc70:~# echo STEP4-DONE; exit
STEP4-DONE
logout

< cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env; echo RC:$?
STEP4-ENV
## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="BmP4QlesfqlwdO9rQRtNw8yN6WLLQUNb"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
RC:0
root@988e22bafc70:~# echo STEP4-DONE; exit
STEP4-DONE
logout

## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="BmP4QlesfqlwdO9rQRtNw8yN6WLLQUNb"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
--- exit=0

CREATE TABLE `_mikro_migrations` (`id` integer not null primary key autoincrement, `name` varchar(255), `executed_at` datetime default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP);
CREATE TABLE sqlite_sequence(name,seq);
CREATE TABLE `Daemon` (`id` text not null, `created_at` datetime not null default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, `updated_at` datetime not null default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, `slug` text not null, `url` text not null, primary key (`id`));
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX `Daemon_slug_unique` on `Daemon` (`slug`);
CREATE TABLE `Identity` (`id` text not null, `created_at` datetime not null default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, `updated_at` datetime not null default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, `slug` text not null, `authentication` json not null default '{}', primary key (`id`));
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX `Identity_slug_unique` on `Identity` (`slug`);

** environment

| fact                | value                                                                  |
|---------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| image               | =alpine:3.20= (digest =sha256:d9e853e87e55526f6b2917df91a2115c36dd7c696a35be12163d44e6e2a4b6bc=) |
| container           | =readmen-alpine= (no published ports; left RUNNING with the instance up) |
| =/etc/os-release=   | =NAME="Alpine Linux"= · =ID=alpine= · =VERSION_ID=3.20.10= · =PRETTY_NAME="Alpine Linux v3.20"= |
| arch                | =aarch64= · kernel =Linux 6.6.26-linuxkit aarch64= · libc = musl        |
| docker              | server 26.1.1 / client 26.1.1                                          |
| clone HEAD          | =b27e0e62e2cde2ba5d80eaeed16d62db9de6c3f9= — crackedbeefcake, Tue Sep 1 19:34:29 2026 +0200, "fix docs" |
| README identity     | =sha256 c9cb142434302783553e296000c82d5d10b1527940ef57d6e90ad6c2170a9132=, 268 lines — byte-identical to the ground-truth copy on the host |
| deno                | =2.9.6 (stable, release, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu)= / v8 15.0.245.2-rusty / typescript 6.0.3 |

*** how deno was obtained

The README's line 1 was run verbatim and *succeeded* — it printed "Deno was installed
successfully", exit 0 — but the binary it installs is =aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu= and Alpine has
no glibc, so it cannot execute at all. Remediation ladder, in the order tried:

| # | attempt                                                    | result |
|---+------------------------------------------------------------+--------|
| a | =apk add gcompat libstdc++ libgcc=, rerun the binary       | FAIL — gets past most of glibc, dies on =Error relocating /root/.deno/bin/deno: __res_init: symbol not found= |
| b | =apk add deno= (alpine 3.20 community)                     | FAIL twice — the repo only carries *deno 1.43.5*, far below the README's 2.7 minimum, and apk cannot even unpack it: =ERROR: Failed to create usr/bin/deno: I/O error= |
| c1| alpine =edge/community= repo                                | carries deno 2.7.4-r3, but it is =masked in: @edge= and wants =libicui18n.so.78=, =libicuuc.so.78=, =libsimdutf.so.35= — none of which exist in 3.20. =apk fetch= of the same package also dies with =IO ERROR=, while plain =curl= of the identical .apk URL succeeds (40 637 317 bytes), so the apk fetcher itself is the thing failing, not the network or the disk (a 200 MB =dd= into =/usr/bin= succeeds). |
| c2| upstream deno release assets                                | there is *no musl build at all*: v2.9.6 publishes only =*-apple-darwin=, =*-pc-windows-msvc=, =*-unknown-linux-gnu= |
| c3| *the bundled glibc from =denoland/deno:alpine-2.9.6=*       | **WORKS.** Deno's own Alpine image is not a musl build either — it ships the same gnu binary plus a private glibc at =/usr/local/lib/glibc= and an =/etc/ld.so.conf= + =ld.so.cache= pointing at it. Transplanting those 21 shared objects + the two loader files into the container makes the README-installed binary run: =deno 2.9.6=. |

*Every step after step 0 ran on that Deno* — the exact binary the README's installer downloads,
executing against denoland's own bundled glibc.

*** packages added beyond what the README names

The README says "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites; the repo carries everything else."
On stock =alpine:3.20= the following were missing and had to be installed:

| package                        | why |
|--------------------------------+-----|
| =curl=, =ca-certificates=      | README line 1 is a =curl= pipeline; neither exists on the image (busybox has only =wget=) |
| =git=                          | README line 3 |
| =bash=                         | the README's shell examples and its =~/.bashrc= advice assume bash; Alpine's =/bin/sh= is busybox ash |
| =unzip=                        | already present as a busybox applet — *not* an addition |
| =gcompat=, =libstdc++=, =libgcc= | remediation attempt (a); left installed, harmless |
| =expect=                       | NOT a README need — the walk's own pty driver for the Ink/cliffy wizards |
| =openssl=                      | the instance's own =.env= comment says "Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24"; absent on the image |
| =sqlite=                       | NOT a README need — used only to read the identity table back for the step-6 checklist |

*No build toolchain was needed.* =python3=, =make=, =g++=, =gcc= were never installed and
=command -v= confirms none are present at the end of the walk. Nothing invoked node-gyp:
=better-sqlite3= has no =build/Release= directory at all, and every native dependency that
mattered resolved to a prebuilt =.node=. The native-module breakage on Alpine was *not*
node-gyp — see defect 3.

*** verdict

=PASS-WITH-DEVIATION= — every README line eventually ran and =deno task install= succeeded in
25 s, but the README's line 1 silently installs an unrunnable binary on musl and its PATH advice
is false on this distro; both needed out-of-band repair.

*** readme delta

- "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites" — on Alpine you additionally need =curl=,
  =ca-certificates=, =bash= (for the README's own shell advice), and a working glibc, which
  Alpine cannot supply from its own repositories at any version ≥ 2.7.
- =curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh= exits 0 and prints "installed successfully"
  while leaving a binary that cannot execute. Nothing in the README warns of this.
- "open a fresh shell or =$ source ~/.bashrc=" — the installer creates no =~/.bashrc=,
  =~/.bash_profile=, =~/.profile= or =~/.deno/env=, so a fresh shell has *no* =viva= and there
  is nothing to source. The PATH export survives only within the one shell that ran it.
- "the repo carries everything else" — true for the JS side; false for the native side on musl
  (see step 4).
- The README's later "=~/vivalence=" shorthand is printed by the tool as the absolute
  =/root/vivalence=; cosmetic.

** step 1 — =viva ledger/init=

*** commands

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
viva ledger/init                      # under a pty, every default accepted with bare Enter
viva ledger/doctor
cat ~/.viva/.env
viva ledger/init < /dev/null          # NOT in the README: the headless path
#+END_EXAMPLE

key pacing):

*** verdict

=PASS= — the wizard runs under a pty, accepts defaults, creates the ledger, and the headless
path degrades gracefully.

*** readme delta

- The README prints a 6-line doctor block. The real =ledger/doctor= prints ten sections
  (=identity=, =scopes=, =environment=, =registry=, =processes=, =instances=, =instance=,
  =locks=, =logs=, =vip cache=) — roughly 45 lines. The scopes section itself matches
  glyph-for-glyph *except* that the README's =✗ instance        — see steps #3-#5= carries a
  hint the tool does not print: reality is just =✗ instance       —=.
- README paths are =~/.viva= / =~/vivalence=; the tool prints =/root/.viva= / =/root/vivalence=.
- "Creates the =ledger= — the one directory Vivalence owns" is only partly true: the *first*
  =viva= invocation (the =ledger/doctor= the README suggests as a smoke test in step 0) already
  created =~/.viva/= with =registry/= and =registry.json= inside, and =ledger/doctor= already
  reported =✓ ledger=. =ledger/init= adds the other five entries.
- The README never mentions that =ledger/init= also writes =VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT= into
  =~/.config/viva/env=; it does.
- Headless =ledger/init= reports =persisted false= — it scaffolds but does not persist the
  mount. The README documents no headless contract at all.

*** readme delta

- *Mode count is wrong.* README: =~/vivalence/registry/viva    @viva    11    @viva/package/viva=.
  Reality: =10=. =@fixtures 3= and =@playground 10= match.
- The README's table shows full unelided mounts and identifiers. The tool elides both to fit an
  80-column terminal (=…vivalence/registry/fixtures=,
  =…ayground/package/playground=), so the README's block is not reproducible as printed.
- The usage string says =viva registry tap= (space) while the verb the README teaches, and the
  one that works, is =viva registry/tap= (slash).
- =registry.json= records the tapped remote as the bare string ="registry-education"= while the
  three seeded packages are absolute paths — two different shapes in one array.

*** verdict

=FAIL= as written, =PASS-WITH-DEVIATION= after one env var. The README's literal step 4 hangs
forever on Alpine: the kajuit child cannot start and =instance/init= has no failure path for a
child that never announces itself.

*** readme delta

- "It runs the instance once. It asks you to create the first user." — on Alpine it runs the
  instance *half* once and never asks, because =Init.jsx= does
  =await Promise.all(processes.map(p => new Promise(resolve => p.out.tap(line => { if
  (line.includes("Status:ALIVE")) resolve() }))))= with no rejection on process exit. A child
  that dies leaves =instance/init= in "starting processes…" indefinitely. This is not
  Alpine-specific — any failing child does it.
- "To sign up additional users, use =viva instance/lighthouse signup <username> <password>= — it
  talks to the running lighthouse, so the instance has to be up (step 5)." The README is
  *correct* about the requirement, but the tool's failure voice never mentions it: you get a
  =TypeError: fetch failed= stack and =ghost: Network request failed=, with no hint that the
  instance is simply down.
- Every boot log line is prefixed with the literal string =undefined= (the Init view renders
  =`${process.spec.type}  ${line}`= and =spec.type= is undefined).
- =instance/doctor= truncates the env table's key column to 12 characters, so six distinct rows
  all read =PUBLIC_VIVA_= and two read =SECRET_VIVA_=. The README promises "an overview of the
  environment"; the overview is unreadable at 80 columns.
- The README's =instances/use hello-world= bind is pid-scoped (it writes
  =~/.viva/sessions/<pid>.json= keyed on =$PPID=). This is nowhere stated, and it means the
  README's step-4 recipe silently does not carry into any later shell. Session files accumulate
  (5 stale files by the end of this walk) and are never reaped, so a recycled pid could
  inherit another shell's selection.

Stop / per-process runs (full transcript in =step5b.log=):

  :2501/status=200  :1794=200  locks=[hello-world_kajuit.lock hello-world_runtime.lock ]

*** verdict

=PASS= — every claim in step 5 holds: the bare form refuses with one line and exit 1, the run
brings both children up in 5 s, =/= is a 404 by design, =/status= is =ALIVE=, per-child runs
write only their own lock, =bogus= is rejected cleanly, and =stop= clears everything with no
survivors.

*** readme delta

- The README's expected block is:
  #+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
  run runtime=8924 kajuit=8925
    VITE v6.3.3  ready in 1467 ms
    ➜  Local:   http://127.0.0.1:1794/
  launching on http://localhost:2501/
  Status:ALIVE
  #+END_EXAMPLE
  Reality differs in four ways: two =Task …= lines and two SvelteKit deprecation warnings appear
  between line 1 and the VITE banner; a first =Status:ALIVE= is printed *before* the VITE banner,
  not after it; the vite address is =http://localhost:1794/=, not =http://127.0.0.1:1794/=; and
  after the README's last line the tool prints a third variant, =$[runtime:runtime] Status:ALIVE=,
  plus a =[vite-plugin-svelte] … hydratable … has been removed= warning. A newcomer diffing
  against the README will not find the block it shows.
- The README's =/status= example timestamp is from the previous day's build; the shape matches
  exactly.
- The README does not say =instance/run= never returns, nor that =instance/run bogus= exits 1
  while =instance/stop= with nothing running exits 0. Both are sane; both are undocumented.
