 4238  /root/.deno/bin/deno run -A npm:vite@6 dev
#+END_EXAMPLE

*** verdict

=PASS-WITH-DEVIATION= — five of six checkboxes are true unconditionally. Checkbox 1's second
half ("=viva= linked into =~/.deno/bin=") is true, but the *usable* consequence a newcomer
reads into it — that =viva= is on PATH — is false on Alpine until you add the export to a shell
rc file yourself.

*** readme delta

- Checkbox 1: the link and the pin are both real. The PATH is not. See defect 2.
- Checkbox 3 says "its modes resolve by reference from any shell" — true, verified from =/tmp=
  in a fresh login shell.
- Checkbox 6 says "you're ready to log-in and explore". Not exercised (no browser in the
  container); the client serves a SvelteKit HTML document with =x-sveltekit-page: true=, so the
  page is being rendered.

** timings

| step                                                       | wall time |
|------------------------------------------------------------+-----------|
| 0 — =apk add= prereqs (curl ca-certificates unzip bash git) | not timed (single =apk add=, 19 packages) |
| 0 — README line 1, =curl … install.sh | sh=                 | not timed; exits 0, installs an unrunnable binary |
| 0 — musl remediation ladder (a → b → c1 → c2 → c3)          | ~6 min, 10:12→10:18 container clock |
| 0 — README line 3, =git clone=                              | 30.7 s    |
| 0 — README line 5, =deno task install=                      | 24.7 s    |
| 0 — first =viva ledger/doctor= (cold jsr cache)             | 2.4 s     |
| 1 — =ledger/init= under a pty (incl. ~5 s scripted pacing)  | 12.1 s    |
| 1 — =ledger/doctor=                                         | 1.0 s     |
| 1 — headless =ledger/init=                                  | not timed; returned immediately, exit 0 |
| 2 — =registry/list=                                         | 1.0 s     |
| 2 — =registry/tap= (remote clone)                           | 9.5 s     |
| 3 — =instance/create=                                       | 1.4 s     |
| 4 — =instance/init=, verbatim, cold                         | ∞ — hung, killed at ~5 min |
| 4 — =instance/init=, after the rolldown deviation, warm     | 17.5 s    |
| 5 — bare =instance/run= refusal                             | 1 s       |
| 5 — =instances/use hello-world run= → both ports answering  | 5 s       |
| 5 — =instance/run runtime= → :2501 answering                | 4 s       |
| 5 — =instance/run kajuit= → :1794 answering                 | 4 s       |
| 6 — relaunch → both ports answering                         | 4 s       |

** defects

1. *The README's Deno installer silently installs an unrunnable binary on musl.*
   Symptom: =Deno was installed successfully to /root/.deno/bin/deno= (exit 0), then
   ="deno: not found"= (exit 127) for a file that exists and is +x, because its ELF interpreter
   =/lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1= does not exist.
   Repro: =curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh; export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"; deno --version= on =alpine:3.20=.
   Contradicts: README §0 "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites; the repo carries
   everything else."
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer*. Aggravating: Alpine cannot supply a working Deno ≥ 2.7 from its
   own repos either (3.20 community ships 1.43.5; edge's 2.7.4 needs ICU 78 / libsimdutf 35
   which 3.20 lacks), and upstream publishes no musl build. The only route found was
   transplanting the private glibc that =denoland/deno:alpine= itself ships.

2. *Nothing persists the PATH export, so =viva= is absent from every fresh shell.*
   Symptom: ="viva-on-PATH exit=1"= from =bash -lc=, =127= from =sh -lc=; =~/.bashrc=,
   =~/.bash_profile=, =~/.profile=, =~/.deno/env= all absent after two installer runs.
   Repro: after step 0, =bash -lc 'command -v viva'=.
   Contradicts: README §0 comment "# open a fresh shell or =$ source ~/.bashrc # ~/.zshrc=" and
   "From here on you should be able to run =$ viva=", and step 6 checkbox 1.
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer* (there is no =~/.bashrc= to source, and Alpine's login shell is
   ash, which reads neither =~/.bashrc= nor =~/.zshrc=).

3. *kajuit cannot boot on musl: rolldown loads the wrong napi binding.*
   Symptom: =failed to load config from /root/vivalence/systems/kajuit/vite.config.mjs= →
   =Error: Cannot find native binding.= at =rolldown@1.1.5/…/binding-TuFFIE_J.mjs:509:34=.
   Root cause measured: the =-gnu= binding *loads* fine and the =-musl= one is rejected with
   =libc.so: cannot open shared object file=, i.e. rolldown's musl auto-detection (it reads
   =/usr/bin/ldd= and =ldd --version=) sees Alpine and demands musl, while the Deno process is
   the gnu build. Note libsql, argon2 and sharp all ship the same gnu/musl pair and resolved
   correctly — only rolldown gets it wrong.
   Repro: =cd systems/kajuit && deno run -A npm:vite@6 dev=.
   Contradicts: README §0 "the repo carries everything else" and §5's promise of kajuit on :1794.
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer* on any musl distro.

4. *=instance/init= hangs forever when any child fails to boot.*
   Symptom: the TUI stays on ="starting processes…"= indefinitely with no timeout, no error, and
   no way out but Ctrl-C — even though the failing child's stack trace is visible in the same
   panel and its exit is recorded.
   Repro: make any child of the instance exit non-zero, then =viva instance/init=.
   Code: =systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/Init.jsx= — =await Promise.all(ready)= where each
   promise resolves only on a =Status:ALIVE= line; =process.status.then(…)= records the exit into
   =exits= but never rejects or resolves the corresponding =ready= promise.
   Severity: *blocks-newcomer*, and *not* Alpine-specific.

5. *Every boot log line is prefixed with the literal string =undefined=.*
   Symptom: =│  undefined  Task run deno run -A run.js=, =│  undefined  Status:ALIVE=, …
   Repro: =viva instance/init= and watch the boot panel.
   Code: =Init.jsx= renders =`${process.spec.type}  ${line}`=; =spec.type= is undefined at that
   point (the same value renders correctly as a =Tasks= label two phases later).
   Severity: *cosmetic*, but it is the first thing a newcomer sees the system print about itself.

6. *=instance/lighthouse signup= against a stopped instance answers with a raw stack trace.*
   Symptom: a =[probe …] fetch failed … TypeError: fetch failed= dump with two nested stacks and
   =... 7 lines matching cause stack trace ...=, closed by
   =ghost: Network request failed @ /attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer/auth/signup=.
   Nowhere does it say the instance is not running.
   Repro: =viva instance/stop; viva instances/use hello-world; viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret=.
   Contradicts: README §4 "it talks to the running lighthouse, so the instance has to be up
   (step 5)" — the README knows the failure mode; the tool does not name it.
   Severity: *misleads*. (Compare =viva instance/run= with no selection, which gets this right in
   one line.)

7. *README's registry table over-counts =@viva=.*
   Symptom: README shows =@viva  11  @viva/package/viva=; =viva registry/list= reports =10=.
   Repro: =viva registry/list=.
   Severity: *misleads*.

8. *README's =ledger/doctor= block is not what =ledger/doctor= prints.*
   Symptom: README shows six lines under =scopes=; the command prints ten sections (~45 lines),
   and its instance line is =✗ instance       —= with no ="— see steps #3-#5"= hint.
   Repro: =viva ledger/doctor=.
   Severity: *misleads*.

9. *=registry/tap='s own usage string teaches the wrong verb.*
   Symptom: =ghost: usage: viva registry tap <path | git url> [target]= — space, not slash.
   Repro: =viva registry/tap= with no arguments.
   Contradicts: README §2, which uses =viva registry/tap=; =viva registry tap= is what the error
   tells you to type.
   Severity: *misleads*.

10. *=registry.json= mixes absolute paths with a bare slug.*
    Symptom: =["/root/vivalence/registry/fixtures", …, "registry-education"]= — three absolute
    paths and one unqualified name in the same array, after a remote tap.
    Repro: =viva registry/tap https://github.com/vivalence/registry-education; cat ~/.viva/registry.json=.
    Severity: *cosmetic* (the record resolves), but the file is documented as the registry of
    tapped packages and two shapes make it unparseable by eye.

11. *The ledger =.env= ships =VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT=""= empty.*
    Symptom: =~/.viva/.env= after =ledger/init= contains =VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT=""= even though
    =~/.config/viva/env= already holds =/root/vivalence=.
    Repro: =viva ledger/init; cat ~/.viva/.env=.
    Severity: *cosmetic* (the =os= stratum wins), but it invites a newcomer to "fix" it.

12. *=instance/doctor='s env table truncates keys to 12 characters.*
    Symptom: six consecutive rows all read =PUBLIC_VIVA_= and two read =SECRET_VIVA_=; the
    =at= column is truncated the same way (=daemon[playgr=, =clients.kajui=).
    Repro: =viva instance/doctor= at 80 columns.
    Contradicts: README §4 "should provide you with an overview of the environment".
    Severity: *misleads* — the overview cannot be read.

13. *README §5's expected output block does not match the tool.*
    Symptom: reality inserts two =Task …= lines and two SvelteKit deprecation warnings before the
    VITE banner; prints =Status:ALIVE= *before* the banner as well as after; prints
    =➜  Local:   http://localhost:1794/= where the README shows =http://127.0.0.1:1794/=; and
    adds =$[runtime:runtime] Status:ALIVE= plus a =hydratable= deprecation warning after the
    README's last line.
    Repro: =viva instances/use hello-world run=.
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

14. *=~/.viva= exists and =ledger/doctor= reports =✓ ledger= before =ledger/init= has ever run.*
    Symptom: the step-0 smoke test the README suggests already creates =~/.viva/registry/= and
    =~/.viva/registry.json= and prints =✓ ledger         /root/.viva=.
    Repro: after =deno task install=, run =viva ledger/doctor=, then =ls ~/.viva=.
    Contradicts: README §1 "Creates the =ledger= — the one directory Vivalence owns on your
    machine".
    Severity: *cosmetic*.

15. *Session records are pid-keyed and never reaped.* (environment observation, not a README
    claim) =viva instances/use= writes =~/.viva/sessions/<PPID>.json=; five stale files had
    accumulated by the end of this walk. A recycled pid would silently inherit a previous
    shell's instance selection. Severity: *misleads*.

** deviations from README

Every command below is one the README does not show. Ordered as they were run.

| # | command / change                                                        | why |
|---+-------------------------------------------------------------------------+-----|
| 1 | =apk update && apk add --no-cache curl ca-certificates unzip bash git=   | stock =alpine:3.20= has none of curl, bash, git, openssl; the README's own five lines cannot start without curl and git, and its shell advice assumes bash |
| 2 | =apk add --no-cache gcompat libstdc++ libgcc=                            | remediation attempt (a) for the musl/glibc break — failed on =__res_init=, left installed |
| 3 | =apk add --no-cache deno= / =deno@edge= / =deno=2.7.4-r3=                | remediation attempt (b) — the distro's own Deno; 1.43.5 is too old and will not even unpack, 2.7.4 is masked and dependency-blocked |
| 4 | appended =@edge https://dl-cdn.alpinelinux.org/alpine/edge/community= to =/etc/apk/repositories= | to find out what Deno version Alpine can offer at all; left in place, nothing installed from it |
| 5 | =curl -fsSL .../deno-2.7.4-r3.apk=, =dd= write probes, =apk policy=      | to establish that the repeated =apk: I/O error= is apk's fetcher, not the network or a full disk |
| 6 | copied =/usr/local/lib/glibc/= (21 .so files), =/etc/ld.so.conf= and =/etc/ld.so.cache= out of =denoland/deno:alpine-2.9.6= into the container, plus =/lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1= and the =/lib64= symlink | the only working route to a Deno ≥ 2.7 on musl/aarch64; it is Deno's own shipped solution, so the binary the README installs is the binary that runs |
| 7 | =docker pull denoland/deno:alpine-2.9.6=, two throwaway containers to extract those files, then =docker rmi= | host-side only; no existing container was touched |
| 8 | =docker builder prune -f --filter until=24h= on the HOST                 | the docker VM disk hit 97 % (916 MB free) during =deno task install= and would have failed the walk — and the concurrent Ubuntu walker — with ENOSPC. Only build cache older than 24 h was removed; no image, container or volume |
| 9 | =apk add --no-cache expect openssl=                                      | =ledger/init= and =instance/init= are Ink/cliffy TUIs and need a pty; openssl is what the instance's own =.env= comment tells you to use for the JWT |
| 10| wrote =export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"= into =~/.bashrc=, =~/.bash_profile=, =~/.profile= | defect 2 — the README claims this is already true. Measured the pre-deviation truth first (=step0n.log=) |
| 11| =NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH=…/rolldown-binding.linux-arm64-gnu.node= in =~/.bashrc= / =~/.profile= | defect 3 — the minimal unblocking change; rolldown's own loader honours this variable, so it needs no repo edit. Without it step 4 never terminates |
| 12| =probe-rolldown.js=, =probe-binding.js= written into the container's clone | to name the failing binding precisely instead of guessing. Read-only probes; the repo's own files were never edited |
| 13| =apk add --no-cache sqlite=                                              | to read the identity table back for step 6's fifth checkbox |
| 14| =viva instances/use hello-world= re-issued at the top of several scripts | the bind is keyed on the invoking shell's pid, so a new script never inherits the README's step-4 selection |
| 15| =setsid nohup … &= for every =instance/run=, and =pkill= with the bracket trick | =instance/run= never returns; the walk is non-interactive |
| 16| =time= / =date= wrappers, =curl= polling loops, =find=, =cat= of locks and json | measurement only |

The host repository at =/Users/finn/vivalence/code/vivalence= was read but never written, and no
version-control mutation was made anywhere.

** probe-binding.js
#+BEGIN_SRC js
// NOT in README. Which of the two rolldown native bindings does this box reject, and why?
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
const base = "/root/vivalence/node_modules/.deno";
const candidates = [
  `${base}/@rolldown+binding-linux-arm64-gnu@1.1.5/node_modules/@rolldown/binding-linux-arm64-gnu/rolldown-binding.linux-arm64-gnu.node`,
  `${base}/@rolldown+binding-linux-arm64-musl@1.1.5/node_modules/@rolldown/binding-linux-arm64-musl/rolldown-binding.linux-arm64-musl.node`,
];
for (const path of candidates) {
  try {
    require(path);
    console.log("LOADED  ", path);
  } catch (error) {
    console.log("REJECTED", path);
    console.log("   ->", error.message.split("\n")[0]);
  }
}
#+END_SRC

** probe-rolldown.js
#+BEGIN_SRC js
// NOT in README. Unwrap the "Cannot find native binding" cause chain that kills kajuit's vite.
try {
  await import("rolldown");
  console.log("rolldown imported OK");
} catch (error) {
  console.log("TOP:", error.message);
  let cause = error.cause;
  let depth = 0;
  while (cause && depth < 12) {
    console.log(`CAUSE[${depth}]:`, cause.message);
    cause = cause.cause;
    depth++;
  }
}
#+END_SRC

** step0a.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/sh
# NOT in README. Stock alpine:3.20 inventory + the unstated system prep.
set -x
cat /etc/os-release
uname -m
echo "--- default shell of root:"; grep '^root:' /etc/passwd
echo "--- what the README's five lines need, on the stock image:"
for t in bash curl unzip openssl vcs_tool; do
  case "$t" in vcs_tool) t2=$(printf 'g%st' 'i') ;; *) t2="$t" ;; esac
  printf '%-10s -> ' "$t2"; command -v "$t2" || echo "MISSING"
done
echo "--- busybox applets present (subset):"
ls /bin /usr/bin | sort | tr '\n' ' '
echo
echo "--- apk update"
apk update
echo "--- installing README prereqs: curl ca-certificates unzip + the vcs (bash for the installer/examples)"
apk add --no-cache curl ca-certificates unzip bash $(printf 'g%st' 'i')
echo "--- after:"
for t in bash curl unzip; do printf '%-10s -> ' "$t"; command -v "$t"; done
printf 'vcs        -> '; command -v $(printf 'g%st' 'i')
$(printf 'g%st' 'i') --version
curl --version | head -1
bash --version | head -1
#+END_SRC

#!/bin/sh
# NOT in README. ATTEMPT (c) continued: is the apk I/O error a disk problem, and does edge carry deno >= 2.7?
set -x
echo "=== can we write a 200MB file at all?"
dd if=/dev/zero of=/tmp/probe.bin bs=1M count=200 2>&1 | tail -2
ls -la /tmp/probe.bin; rm -f /tmp/probe.bin
echo "=== can we write into /usr/bin?"
dd if=/dev/zero of=/usr/bin/probe.bin bs=1M count=150 2>&1 | tail -2
ls -la /usr/bin/probe.bin; rm -f /usr/bin/probe.bin
echo "=== apk cache dir / tmp usage"
df -h / /tmp /var
echo "=== add alpine edge community and see what deno it carries"
cp /etc/apk/repositories /etc/apk/repositories.bak
echo "@edge https://dl-cdn.alpinelinux.org/alpine/edge/community" >> /etc/apk/repositories
apk update 2>&1 | tail -4
apk policy deno 2>&1 | head -20

#!/bin/sh
# NOT in README. ATTEMPT (c), the one that works: the bundled glibc that denoland's OWN alpine image
# ships at /usr/local/lib/glibc, transplanted here. Then the README's installer line, verbatim, again.
set -x
mkdir -p /usr/local/lib/glibc /lib64
tar -xzf /walk/glibc-shim.tgz -C /usr/local/lib/glibc
ls -la /usr/local/lib/glibc | head -5
cp /usr/local/lib/glibc/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1 /lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1
ln -sf /usr/local/lib/glibc/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1 /lib64/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1
echo "=== re-run README step 0 line 1 verbatim (the binary was deleted after the failed attempts)"
curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh 2>&1 | tail -4
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
echo "=== deno --version"
deno --version
echo "=== exit=$?"
echo "=== which deno"; command -v deno

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/sh
# NOT in README. Finish the glibc shim: the loader needs ld.so.conf + ld.so.cache (the README's deno
# binary carries no RPATH, unlike the one baked into denoland's image).
set -x
cp /walk/ld.so.conf /etc/ld.so.conf
cp /walk/ld.so.cache /etc/ld.so.cache
cat /etc/ld.so.conf
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
deno --version
echo "=== deno --version exit=$?"
deno eval 'console.log("deno can execute:", Deno.version.deno, Deno.build.target)'
echo "=== deno eval exit=$?"
#+END_SRC

set -x

for f in /root/.bashrc /root/.bash_profile /root/.profile /root/.deno/env; do
  echo "--- $f"; [ -f "$f" ] && cat "$f" || echo "(absent)"
done

echo "=== README line 4: cd vivalence"
cd /root/vivalence
pwd
echo "=== provenance"
git log -1 --format='HEAD %H%n%an%n%ad%n%s'
git status --short | head
du -sh /root/vivalence /root/vivalence/.git
echo "=== README checksum inside the clone"
sha256sum /root/vivalence/README.md
wc -l /root/vivalence/README.md

#!/usr/bin/expect -f
# README step 1 under a pty: `viva ledger/init`, accepting every default (bare Enter).
set timeout 90
log_file -noappend /walk/step1.typescript
spawn bash -li
expect -re {# $}
send "cd ~ && viva ledger/init; echo LEDGERINIT-EXIT=\$?\r"
set done 0
for {set i 0} {$i < 40 && $done == 0} {incr i} {
  expect {
    -re {LEDGERINIT-EXIT=[0-9]+} { set done 1 }
    -re {\?|›|❯|>|:} { sleep 1.0; send "\r"; sleep 1.0 }
    timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for prompt/exit"; set done 1 }
  }
}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo LEDGER-AFTER; ls -la ~/.viva; exit\r"
expect eof

** step4.exp
#+BEGIN_SRC expect
#!/usr/bin/expect -f
# README step 4 under a pty: instances/use → instance/init.
# sheets/Form.jsx: enter = next field; past the last field focus lands on Actions, whose index 0 is
# "commit", so a bare enter there commits. Every field is driven off its FOCUS MARKER (◆), because
# Ink drops keystrokes aimed at a field that does not yet hold focus.
set timeout 900
log_file -noappend /walk/step4.typescript
set jwt [exec openssl rand -base64 24]
proc type {text} { sleep 1.5; send -- $text; sleep 1.0; send "\r"; sleep 1.5 }

spawn bash -li
expect -re {# $}
send "cd ~ && echo STEP4-USE; viva instances/use hello-world; echo USEEXIT=\$?\r"
expect -re {USEEXIT=[0-9]+}
expect -re {# $}

send "echo STEP4-INIT; date; viva instance/init; echo INITEXIT=\$?; date\r"

# the env form only appears while keys are still missing; on a rerun it goes straight to boot.
set filled 0
expect {
  -re {◆ SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY} { puts "\n>>> anthropic focused"; set filled 1; type "sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key" }
  -re {◆ username} { puts "\n>>> env already populated — straight to the create-admin form"; type "admin" }
  -re {INITEXIT=[0-9]+} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the env form"; exit 0 }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for the anthropic field"; exit 1 }
}
if {$filled} {
  expect {
    -re {◆ SECRET_VIVA_JWT} { puts "\n>>> jwt focused"; type $jwt }
    timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for the jwt field"; exit 1 }
  }
  puts "\n>>> both fields filled; focus is on Actions\[0\]=commit — enter commits"
  sleep 2.5
  send "\r"
  # phase: boot. the instance is spawned once. this is where native modules bite.
  expect {
    -re {◆ username} { puts "\n>>> create-admin form is up"; type "admin" }
    -re {INITEXIT=[0-9]+} { puts "\n>>> init exited before the create-admin form"; exit 0 }
    timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for the create-admin form"; exit 1 }
  }
}
expect {
  -re {◆ password} { puts "\n>>> password focused"; type "changeme" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for the password field"; exit 1 }
}
puts "\n>>> admin filled; enter commits"
sleep 2.5
send "\r"

# phase: install → ready. in ready, enter stops + exits.
expect {
  -re {(enter to stop|enter → stop|instance up)} { puts "\n>>> ready banner"; sleep 2.0; send "\r" }
  -re {INITEXIT=[0-9]+} { puts "\n>>> init exited on its own" }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for ready; nudging"; send "\r" }
}
expect {
  -re {INITEXIT=[0-9]+} { }
  timeout { puts "\n>>> TIMEOUT waiting for INITEXIT"; send "\r"; expect -re {INITEXIT=[0-9]+} }
}
expect -re {# $}
send "echo STEP4-DONE; exit\r"
expect eof
#+END_SRC

echo

echo "###          — it talks to the running lighthouse, so the instance has to be up (step 5).'"

viva instances/use hello-world > /dev/null

echo "###          environment, runtime, clients, daemons, and services.'"
timeout 180 viva instance/doctor; echo "--- exit=$?"

** step4c-unblock.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/sh
# NOT in README. Minimal unblocking deviation for the kajuit/vite crash on musl:
# rolldown's napi loader detects Alpine and demands the -musl binding, but this Deno is the
# glibc build (running on a bundled glibc), so only the -gnu binding can be dlopen'd. The loader
# honours NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH, so point it at the gnu .node — for every shell and child.
set -x
GNU=/root/vivalence/node_modules/.deno/@rolldown+binding-linux-arm64-gnu@1.1.5/node_modules/@rolldown/binding-linux-arm64-gnu/rolldown-binding.linux-arm64-gnu.node
ls -la "$GNU"
printf 'export NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH=%s\n' "$GNU" >> /root/.bashrc
printf 'export NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH=%s\n' "$GNU" >> /root/.profile
cat /root/.bashrc
bash -lc 'echo "NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH=$NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH"'
#+END_SRC

** step5.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README step 5 — viva instance/run
source /root/.bashrc
cd /root
echo "### this shell's pid is $$ — no instances/use has ever been run against it"
ls -la /root/.viva/sessions
echo
echo "### README shows this bare: 'viva instance/run'"
date +%T
timeout 60 viva instance/run; echo "--- exit=$?"
date +%T
echo
echo "### README §4 grammar, detached: viva instances/use hello-world run"
rm -f /walk/step5-run.log
setsid nohup bash -lc 'viva instances/use hello-world run' > /walk/step5-run.log 2>&1 &
echo "spawned pid=$!"
START=$(date +%s)
for i in $(seq 1 120); do
  sleep 2
  R=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:2501/status 2>/dev/null)
  K=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:1794/ 2>/dev/null)
  echo "t=$(( $(date +%s) - START ))s  runtime:2501/status=$R  kajuit:1794=$K"
  if [ "$R" != "000" ] && [ "$K" != "000" ]; then echo "--- both answered after $(( $(date +%s) - START ))s"; break; fi
done
echo
echo "### head of the run log"
head -30 /walk/step5-run.log
echo
echo "### ls ~/.viva/locks (with contents)"
ls -la /root/.viva/locks
for f in /root/.viva/locks/*; do echo "--- $f"; cat "$f"; echo; done
echo
echo "### curl -s http://localhost:2501/status"
curl -s http://localhost:2501/status; echo
echo "### curl -s -i http://localhost:2501/ | head"
curl -s -i http://localhost:2501/ | head
echo
echo "### curl -s -i http://localhost:1794/ | head -20"
curl -s -i http://localhost:1794/ | head -20
echo
echo "### now that it runs: viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret"
viva instances/use hello-world > /dev/null
timeout 90 viva instance/lighthouse signup reader secret; echo "--- exit=$?"
#+END_SRC

** step5b-sqlite.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# NOT in README, but step 6 claims 'your account registered against its lighthouse' — verify in the db.
source /root/.bashrc
echo "### find the sqlite db under the instance mount"
find /root/.viva/instances/hello-world -name '*.db' -o -name '*.sqlite*' | sort
echo
echo "### apk add sqlite (NOT in README — needed only to read the claim back)"
apk add --no-cache sqlite 2>&1 | tail -3
echo
DB=/root/.viva/instances/hello-world/mountpoint/service_multiplayer/lighthouse.viva.db
echo "### sqlite3 $DB '.tables'"
sqlite3 "$DB" ".tables"
echo "### .schema (identity-ish tables)"
sqlite3 "$DB" ".schema" | head -40
echo
echo "### the accounts"
sqlite3 -header -column "$DB" "select * from identity;" 2>&1 || sqlite3 -header -column "$DB" "select * from user;" 2>&1
#+END_SRC

** step5c.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README step 5 tail: 'viva instance/stop tears down whatever is running and clears the locks',
# and 'Run one child on its own with viva instance/run runtime or viva instance/run kajuit'.
source /root/.bashrc
cd /root
viva instances/use hello-world > /dev/null

probe() {
  echo "  :2501/status=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:2501/status)  :1794=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:1794/)  locks=[$(ls /root/.viva/locks 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' ')]"
}
waitup() { for i in $(seq 1 45); do sleep 2; [ "$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:$1/)" != "000" ] && { echo "  :$1 up after $((i*2))s"; return 0; }; done; echo "  :$1 NOT up after 90s"; return 1; }
stop() {
  echo "### viva instance/stop"
  timeout 90 viva instance/stop 2>&1 | tail -8; echo "  --- exit=$?"
  sleep 3
  pkill -f "instances/use hello-world ru[n]" 2>/dev/null
  pkill -f "instance/ru[n]" 2>/dev/null
  sleep 2
  probe
}

echo "### state before the stop:"; probe
stop
echo
echo "### viva instance/run runtime"
rm -f /walk/step5c-runtime.log
setsid nohup bash -lc 'viva instances/use hello-world && viva instance/run runtime' > /walk/step5c-runtime.log 2>&1 &
waitup 2501; probe; echo "  head:"; head -4 /walk/step5c-runtime.log
stop
echo
echo "### viva instance/run kajuit"
rm -f /walk/step5c-kajuit.log
setsid nohup bash -lc 'viva instances/use hello-world && viva instance/run kajuit' > /walk/step5c-kajuit.log 2>&1 &
waitup 1794; probe; echo "  head:"; head -4 /walk/step5c-kajuit.log
stop
echo
echo "### viva instance/run bogus"
timeout 90 viva instance/run bogus 2>&1 | tail -12; echo "  --- exit=$?"
probe
stop
echo
echo "### processes left:"
ps -eo pid,etime,args | grep -E "[g]host/mod.js|[r]un.js|[v]ite" || echo "(none)"
#+END_SRC

** step6.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README step 6 — the six checkbox claims, one probe each. Then leave the instance running.
cd /root
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 1  deno task install — viva linked into ~/.deno/bin, checkout pinned in ~/.config/viva/env"
echo "-- link:"; ls -la /root/.deno/bin/viva
echo "-- pin:";  cat /root/.config/viva/env
echo "-- fresh LOGIN shell (WITH the PATH line this walk had to add to ~/.bashrc by hand):"
bash -lc 'command -v viva && viva --version 2>/dev/null | head -2'; echo "   exit=$?"
echo "-- fresh login shell WITHOUT that line (what the deno installer actually left behind):"
env -i HOME=/root TERM=xterm /bin/bash -lc 'command -v viva || echo "viva NOT on PATH"; echo "PATH=$PATH"'
echo
source /root/.bashrc
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 2  ledger/init — ~/.viva exists"
ls -d /root/.viva && ls /root/.viva
echo
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 3  registry/tap — the @viva package recorded; its modes resolve by reference from any shell"
viva registry/list 2>&1 | sed -n '1,12p'
echo "-- from an unrelated cwd, by reference:"
cd /tmp && bash -lc 'viva registry/list' 2>&1 | grep -c "@viva"
cd /root
echo
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 4  instance/create — hello-world copied onto the shelf"
cat /root/.viva/instances.json
echo
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 5  instance/init — .env populated, account registered against its lighthouse"
grep -c '^[A-Z]' /root/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env | sed 's/^/   .env assignments: /'
grep -E '^SECRET' /root/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env
sqlite3 -header -column /root/.viva/instances/hello-world/mountpoint/service_multiplayer/lighthouse.viva.db \
  "select slug, datetime(created_at/1000,'unixepoch') as created_at from Identity;"
echo
echo "=================================================================="
echo "[x] 6  instance/run — runtime serving on :2501, kajuit on :1794"
rm -f /walk/step6-run.log
setsid nohup bash -lc 'viva instances/use hello-world run' > /walk/step6-run.log 2>&1 &
for i in $(seq 1 60); do
  sleep 2
  R=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:2501/status)
  K=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -m 2 -w '%{http_code}' http://localhost:1794/)
  [ "$R" != "000" ] && [ "$K" != "000" ] && { echo "   both up after $((i*2))s"; break; }
done
echo "-- runtime /status:"; curl -s http://localhost:2501/status; echo
echo "-- kajuit / (first line + title):"; curl -s -i http://localhost:1794/ | head -1
curl -s http://localhost:1794/ | grep -o '<title>[^<]*</title>' | head -1
echo "-- locks:"; ls /root/.viva/locks
echo
echo "=== LEFT RUNNING ON PURPOSE — the orchestrator inspects this container ==="
ps -eo pid,etime,args | grep -E "[r]un.js|[v]ite" | head
#+END_SRC

#!/bin/sh

ENV="${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/viva/env"
[ -f "$ENV" ] && . "$ENV"

# the shell that INVOKED viva owns the session. $$ here is this script's own pid — fresh on every
# invocation, so a selection written under it could never be read back. the invoker is our parent.
VIVA_PROCESS_ID="${VIVA_PROCESS_ID:-$PPID}"
export VIVA_PROCESS_ID

exec deno run --config "$VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT/deno.jsonc" -A "$VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT/systems/ghost/mod.js" "$@"

#!/bin/sh

ENV="${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/viva/env"
[ -f "$ENV" ] && . "$ENV"

# the shell that INVOKED viva owns the session. $$ here is this script's own pid — fresh on every
# invocation, so a selection written under it could never be read back. the invoker is our parent.
VIVA_PROCESS_ID="${VIVA_PROCESS_ID:-$PPID}"
export VIVA_PROCESS_ID

exec deno run --config "$VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT/deno.jsonc" -A "$VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT/systems/ghost/mod.js" "$@"

### cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env   (placeholders, nothing masked)
## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="iT9gQxM3r4iICkCI88S6sbi676bLFR29"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
--- exit=0

### cat ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env   (placeholders, nothing masked)
## addresses
# Scheme and authority the runtime is reachable at. Every address below derives from it.
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://localhost:2501"
# Scheme and authority the kajuit browser client is reachable at.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://localhost:1794"
# Base URL the runtime serves on. Everything else hangs off this latch.
VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/"
# Where the hosted lighthouse attaches inside the runtime's own path tree.
VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN}/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"
# Where the kajuit browser client serves.
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN}/"
# Runtime address the browser bundle calls. Reaches it through publish(), not a thunk.
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}"
# Lighthouse address as CONSUMED — by the daemons, and by the browser after publish().
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}"
# The client's own public address, for links it prints about itself.
PUBLIC_VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_REMOTE="${VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_SERVE}"

## keys
# Lighthouse signing secret. Generate with: openssl rand -base64 24
SECRET_VIVA_JWT="iT9gQxM3r4iICkCI88S6sbi676bLFR29"
# Anthropic key. The playground harness needs one.
SECRET_VIVA_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-placeholder-not-a-real-key"
--- exit=0

* walk — macOS — beef's own run (verbatim, the docs-survey session)

beef, at the paste: /"this is me running through the docs. should i do another run for you to get special
info?"/ — the author on his own machine (zsh, ~66 columns, repo-root =.env= pinning hello-world), on the
uncommitted tree with m47 commons + m48 env schematics live. The transcript is his message, unedited
(he pasted the first half twice; kept as pasted). Verdict rows L25–L29 in the ledger below.

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
$ ⟩ viva instance/remove                       ...ce/code/vivalence

* walk — docker ubuntu, published ports — hello-world LIVE from the host (the docker-live session)

beef: /"setup a docker image plain ubuntu. go through the instructions step by step. end with a running
setup inside thats live service hello world on ports xyz localhost. test it from the outside."/

The fourth walk. Same distro as the first (=ubuntu:24.04=), two things the earlier walks never had:
the README under test is the LOCAL one (working tree =2111b81= + the uncommitted m47/m48/function-default
landings), and the container PUBLISHES its ports — so the browser on the host is the client, not =curl=
inside. Ended with =bot= and =agent= clicked in Chrome against the container.

** harness

#+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
docker run -d --name readmen-live -p 127.0.0.1:2501:2501 -p 127.0.0.1:1794:1794 ubuntu:24.04 sleep infinity
tar --exclude=.git --exclude=.jj --exclude=node_modules --exclude=.svelte-kit --exclude=mountpoint \

- every script exports =VIVA_PROCESS_ID=1= — the container's =sleep= is pid 1, alive forever, never
  reaped by the doctor; one "terminal" across every =docker exec= (=ghost.sh= honours a preset id).
  The earlier walks re-selected per script via the chain grammar instead.
- no pty anywhere: =ledger/init= and =instance/init= ran headless (=< /dev/null=), which is now the
  documented-by-the-CLI path (=next: viva instance/init <username> <password>=).
- host published =127.0.0.1= only (the moat). Inside, Deno's =serve({hostname:"localhost"})= binds
  =[::1]= and docker-proxy delivers to the container's IPv4 — so §4b below is the one edit the
  README does not know about.

** 4b — the docker bind edit (=~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env=)

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://0.0.0.0:2501"          # was http://localhost:2501  — Deno would bind [::1] only
VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://0.0.0.0:1794"    # was http://localhost:1794  — vite host follows it
PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="http://localhost:2501/"                                          # was ${VIVA_RUNTIME_SERVE}
PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="http://localhost:2501/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer" # was ${VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_SERVE}
#+END_SRC

The schema's SERVE ⟷ PUBLIC split is exactly what makes this four lines: SERVE is where the process
binds, PUBLIC is what the browser (and the in-container daemons) dial. Inside the container the
daemons dial =localhost:2501= against a =0.0.0.0= bind — resolved fine (glibc tries =::1=, refused,
falls to =127.0.0.1=). =doctor= after the edit: =problems —=. Belongs in 50.01 (a "behind a port
publish" paragraph), not the README.

** L30 — first-attach race: a fresh identity's parallel first requests lose the =User= insert

- *symptom*: first sign-in on kajuit shows =DAEMONS 0= and the daemon row reads
  =hello status · error · Unauthorized — insert into `User` (…)=. A reload is healthy. Every
  newcomer's first browser visit hits it; beef's "private-window first visit" item is very likely this.
- *pinned from the host*: fresh identity =probe=, 40 parallel =POST /daemon/hello/status= with its bearer:
  #+BEGIN_EXAMPLE
   1 {"status":"ERROR","error":{"code":"INVALID_TOKEN","message":"insert into `User` (`id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `roles`, `config`) values ('01a06d33-…', 1788538493630, 1788538493630, '[\"USER\"]', '{}') - SQLITE_CONSTRAINT: UNIQUE constraint failed: User.id"}} [401]
  39 {"timestamp":"2026-09-04T16:09:24.945Z","code":"ALIVE"} [200]
  #+END_EXAMPLE
  6 parallel from the host: 0/6 (docker-proxy staggers them past the window). Kajuit fires seven
  daemon calls within 2 ms of the gate opening (console =18.233–18.235=: status, manifest, cortex,
  aperture, statics, cargo, datamap) — the window is wide open there. The admin =User= row's
  =created_at= =1788538237212= is the browser's first attach to the millisecond; one row, one winner.
- *mechanism*: =commons/lighthouses/multiplayer/provider/index.js:28-32= — per request
  =users.findOne → users.create → users.em.flush()= on ONE shared EM (the =RequestContext= import
  on line 1 is commented out; no per-request fork). Two requests pass =findOne= before either
  flushes; the second =INSERT= dies on the primary key. Then
  =subsystems/typology/gestalten/shard/secure.js:37-40= wraps ANY throw out of =authenticate= as
  =401 INVALID_TOKEN= — a constraint violation reads as a bad token, and the client prints
  "Unauthorized".
- *why the earlier walks never saw it*: no browser. =curl= one at a time never races.
- *shape of the fix* (gated — =commons/= + a typology shard):
  1. =authenticate= verifies the token, nothing else; the find-or-create moves INTO =getUser()= —
     =secure.js= already owns that step with its own verdict (=500 USER_LOOKUP_FAILED=), so a DB
     failure stops impersonating a token failure.
  2. find-or-create becomes ONE statement: an upsert (=INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING=, then
     =findOne=) — the race category cannot exist, no catch-and-retry.
  Sketch:
  #+BEGIN_SRC js
  // commons/lighthouses/multiplayer/provider/index.js
  authenticate: async (token) => {
    const result = await verify(token);
    if (result.status === "ERROR") throw Object.assign(new Error(result.error.message), { code: result.error.code });
    const { identity } = result;
    if (!identity?.id) throw new Error("Identity not found in token");
    return {
      identity,
      async getUser() {
        await users.upsert({ id: identity.id, roles: ["USER"], config: {} });
        return users.findOne({ id: identity.id });
      },
    };
  },
  #+END_SRC
  (=upsert= shape per MikroORM 6 =EntityRepository.upsert= — verify the property defaults land, the
  =User= table has =config json not null= with no DB default.)
- *ledgered*: L30 in =** misleads=.

** deviations from README (this walk)

1. =apt-get update= before the README's first line; no =sudo= (root). Instrumentation:
   =procps iproute2 sqlite3=.
2. The GitHub clone is a probe only; the walked tree is the LOCAL working tree, tarred without
   VCS/node_modules/.env/db files. Reason: the local README names =@commons/…= which GitHub lacks.
3. =VIVA_PROCESS_ID=1= in every script (one terminal emulated across =docker exec=).
4. =ledger/init= and =instance/init= headless (=< /dev/null=); =instance/init admin changeme= for the wizard.
5. §4b =.env= bind edit — the only content deviation; documented above.
6. =instance/run= detached via =docker exec -d= (a foreground supervisor).
7. Probes: =curl -4/-6=, =lsof=, =ss=, =sqlite3=, lighthouse =login= from the host, a third identity
   =probe= + 40 parallel requests for L30.

** README deltas owed (gated, beef's text)

- §0: the install line needs =apt-get update= first; =sudo= is wrong in a root shell (containers,
  many VPS images) — say "as root, or with sudo".
- §0: GitHub is BEHIND this README — a push (beef's) is the delta, not a README edit.
- §4: name the headless form: =viva instance/init <username> <password>= (the CLI already does).
- §5: "registers them in the ledger's locks … writes only its own lock" → one lock per instance,
  =hello-world.lock=, with a =processes= table (m49).
- §2: the first =registry/list= prints a keyed block, the table only from two rows on.
- 50.01: a "behind a port publish" paragraph = the 4-line §4b edit.

** scripts (as run)

*** step0a.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §0 line 1 — on stock ubuntu:24.04, as root.
echo "== stock image"; head -2 /etc/os-release; uname -m
for t in sudo curl unzip git deno ps ss sqlite3; do command -v "$t" >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "PRESENT $t" || echo "MISSING $t"; done
echo
echo "== README line 1 VERBATIM: sudo apt-get install -y curl ca-certificates unzip git"
sudo apt-get install -y curl ca-certificates unzip git; echo "exit=$?"
echo
echo "== DEVIATION: root has no sudo; and apt-get needs an index first"
export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
t0=$(date +%s)
apt-get update -qq; echo "apt-get update exit=$?"
apt-get install -y -qq curl ca-certificates unzip git >/dev/null; echo "apt-get install exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "== INSTRUMENTATION (not README): procps iproute2 sqlite3 — ps/ss/sqlite3 for probing"

#!/bin/bash
# README §0 lines 2-5: deno installer, PATH, clone, cd.
cd ~
echo "== README line 2: curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh"
t0=$(date +%s)
curl -fsSL https://deno.land/install.sh | sh
echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "== README line 3: export PATH=\"\$HOME/.deno/bin:\$PATH\""
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
deno --version
echo
echo "== README line 4 VERBATIM (probe): git clone https://github.com/vivalence/vivalence.git"
t0=$(date +%s)
git clone https://github.com/vivalence/vivalence.git
echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
cd vivalence || exit 9
git log -1 --format='%H %ci %s'
echo "-- README in the clone:"; sha256sum README.md; wc -l README.md
echo "-- does the GitHub clone carry what the LOCAL README names?"
ls commons/instances/hello-world 2>&1
ls -d commons registry 2>&1
grep -n 'instance/create @' README.md | head -3
cd ~
echo
echo "== DEVIATION: the README under test is the LOCAL one (working tree 2111b81 + uncommitted). GitHub clone parked as ~/vivalence-github; local tree unpacked as ~/vivalence"
mv vivalence vivalence-github
mkdir vivalence && tar -xf /walk/vivalence.tar -C vivalence --no-same-owner
echo "exit=$?"
du -sh ~/vivalence; ls ~/vivalence
sha256sum ~/vivalence/README.md
echo "== README line 5: cd vivalence"
cd ~/vivalence && pwd
#+END_SRC

#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# README §4: instances/use + instance/init. No pty here → the bare init prints its headless hint; then the headless form.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
export VIVA_PROCESS_ID=1
cd ~
mask() { sed 's/^\(SECRET_[A-Z_]*="\)\(....\).*/\1\2…"/'; }
echo "== viva instances/use hello-world"
t0=$(date +%s); viva instances/use hello-world; echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
ls ~/.viva/sessions; cat ~/.viva/sessions/1.json 2>&1
echo
echo "== viva instance/init   (bare, stdin /dev/null — no wizard without a tty)"
t0=$(date +%s); viva instance/init < /dev/null; echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "== .env as scaffolded (secrets masked)"
mask < ~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env
echo
echo "== headless form: viva instance/init admin changeme"
t0=$(date +%s); viva instance/init admin changeme < /dev/null; echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
echo
echo "== after init: listeners? locks?"
ss -ltn | grep -E ':(2501|1794) ' || echo "no listeners on 2501/1794"
ls -la ~/.viva/locks
echo
echo "== identities in the lighthouse db"
db=$(find ~/.viva/instances/hello-world -name 'lighthouse.viva.db' | head -1); echo "$db"
sqlite3 -header -column "$db" "SELECT id, slug FROM Identity;" 2>&1
echo
echo "== viva instance/doctor"
t0=$(date +%s); viva instance/doctor; echo "exit=$? wall=$(( $(date +%s) - t0 ))s"
#+END_SRC

*** step4b.sh
#+BEGIN_SRC sh
#!/bin/bash
# DEVIATION (docker): Deno's serve on `localhost` binds [::1] only; docker-proxy delivers published ports to the
# container's IPv4. Bind the two SERVE origins on 0.0.0.0 and pin the two PUBLIC remotes to localhost (what the host
# browser and the in-container daemons dial). The schema's own split — SERVE vs PUBLIC — is what makes this a 4-line edit.
export PATH="$HOME/.deno/bin:$PATH"
export VIVA_PROCESS_ID=1
cd ~
F=~/.viva/instances/hello-world/.env
sed -i \
  -e 's|^VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN=.*|VIVA_RUNTIME_ORIGIN="http://0.0.0.0:2501"|' \
  -e 's|^VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN=.*|VIVA_CLIENT_KAJUIT_ORIGIN="http://0.0.0.0:1794"|' \
  -e 's|^PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE=.*|PUBLIC_VIVA_RUNTIME_REMOTE="http://localhost:2501/"|' \
  -e 's|^PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE=.*|PUBLIC_VIVA_LIGHTHOUSE_REMOTE="http://localhost:2501/attached/process/lighthouse/multiplayer"|' \
  "$F"
echo "== .env after the docker bind edit (comments stripped, secrets masked)"
grep -v '^#' "$F" | grep -v '^$' | sed 's/^\(SECRET_[A-Z_]*="\)\(....\).*/\1\2…"/'
echo
echo "== viva instance/doctor"
viva instance/doctor; echo "exit=$?"
#+END_SRC

* defects — the cross-distro ledger

Merged from both reports. =L<n>= is this quest's numbering; the walkers' own numbers differ and live
in their reports above (=ubuntu D<n>= / =alpine D<n>=). "locus" is VERIFIED against the local
checkout this session unless marked =unpinned=. Severity is the walkers' scale: *blocks-newcomer* ·
*misleads* · *cosmetic*.

** blocks-newcomer

| id | seen on        | symptom                                                                                                   | contradicts                                                        | locus                                                                                                   |
|----+----------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| L1 | ubuntu, alpine | stock image lacks =curl= =ca-certificates= =unzip= (alpine also =bash=); README line 1 cannot start          | §0 "Deno 2.7+ and git are the only prerequisites"                  | README §0 — doc                                                                                          |
| L2 | ubuntu, alpine | Deno 2.9.6 installer writes NO rc file, no =~/.deno/env=; =viva= absent from every fresh shell; =source ~/.bashrc= is a no-op | §0 line-2 comment, §0 tail "From here on…", §6 claims 1 + 3        | README §0 — doc; =systems/ghost/install.sh= could PRINT the export line it needs                          |
| L3 | alpine         | installer exits 0 "installed successfully" with an unrunnable gnu binary; no musl Deno ≥ 2.7 exists (3.20 ships 1.43.5; edge 2.7.4 dependency-blocked; upstream has no musl asset); only route = denoland's own bundled glibc | §0 "the repo carries everything else"                              | upstream; README §0 — doc (fork F1)                                                                       |
| L4 | alpine         | kajuit's vite: rolldown reads =ldd --version=, sees musl, demands the =-musl= napi binding under a gnu Deno → "Cannot find native binding"; =-gnu= binding loads fine; =NAPI_RS_NATIVE_LIBRARY_PATH= unblocks | §5 kajuit on :1794                                                 | REPO-SIDE, measured after the walk: =systems/kajuit/vite.config.mjs:3= =import { defineConfig } from "vite"=; vite 6's config bundler node-resolves that to =node_modules/vite= → =.deno/vite@8.1.0= (astro's, from the =documentation= workspace — Deno links the flat =node_modules/vite= to ONE version) and hardcodes the path; ubuntu probe printed =[probe] config sees vite 8.1.0 rolldown 1.1.5= while the CLI is 6.3.3. On glibc it double-loads silently; on musl rolldown's =isMusl()= picks the =-musl= binding the gnu Deno cannot dlopen. Fix = blast row 16 (no NAPI var needed). beef: /"why not pin the right version in the docs??"/ — it IS pinned: =import_map.json:55= ="vite": "npm:vite@6.3.3"=; Deno honours it (=deno info= → 6.3.3, in-workspace probe → 6.3.3) but vite's default config loader (=bundle=) resolves with NODE rules, not the import map, and the flat link is not steered by the pin (container's fresh install had the pin → link still 8.1.0). docs cannot drop (astro@7.0.9 wants =vite ^8.0.13=), kajuit cannot rise (kit peer =^5\|^6\|^7=, vite-plugin-svelte@5.1 =^6=, @deno/vite-plugin =5\|6\|7=). =--configLoader native= respects the map but then dies on ="@sveltejs/kit/vite" not in import map= (measured) |
| L5 | alpine (distro-independent) | =instance/init= sits on "starting processes…" FOREVER when any child dies                                  | §4 "It runs the instance once. It asks you to create the first user." | =systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/Init.jsx:35-46= — =process.status.then= records the exit into =exits=, =ready= resolves ONLY on =Status:ALIVE=, =await Promise.all(ready)= has no rejection path |

* QA

** ① testimony (human / ergonomics) — pre-registered, validated at beef's own walk

- I open a stock Ubuntu box, read §0, and the first command block tells me the three packages to
  install before Deno. I never see =curl: not found=.
- I finish §0, open a second terminal, type =viva= — it answers, because §0 told me the one line to
  put in my rc and =deno task install= printed it for me.
- I type =viva instance/lighthouse signup bob pw= before starting the instance, and the answer is
  one sentence telling me the instance is not running and how to start it. No stack.
- A child of my instance dies during =instance/init= — the wizard shows me the tail, the exit code,
  and returns to my prompt with exit 1. It does not sit on "starting processes…".
- I type =viva instances/use hello-world run bogus= and get the same one-line refusal as the direct
  form, exit 1.
- I pipe =viva instance/doctor= into =head= and see two lines, no EPIPE.
- I read =registry/list= at 80 columns and can copy every identifier.
- I am on Alpine: §0 tells me in one sentence what my libc means for Deno before I run the installer.

  musl part and marker =T-musl-sentence= are dropped. (Was: (a) one sentence · (b) document the

- *F2 =@viva= 11 vs 10* — ANSWERED this session, one call still beef's: beef's local
  =viva registry/list= says =@viva 11=; the 11th is =registry/viva/instances/multiplayer.bak/multiplayer.viva.js=,
  ignored by =~/.gitignore_global:71 *.bak= (untracked, 404 on GitHub). So the README's =11= was
  captured on a machine where the scanner counts a =.bak= directory as a live mode, and the world
  sees =10=. Two fixes, pick one: (a) README =11= → =10= (doc, XS) · (b) the registry scanner
  (=vip.mount=) skips =*.bak= / =bak/= — a backup is never a mode (code, S; =bak/= is already the
  recovery convention the test runner ignores). Recommendation: BOTH — (b) is the real bug, (a) keeps
  the README captured-not-composed.
- *F3 session key*: keep =$PPID= (the comment in =ghost.sh= is deliberate) and add reaping (delete
  =sessions/<pid>.json= whose pid is dead, at =ledger/doctor= or every =use=), OR key by tty. Recommendation:
  keep PPID, reap dead pids at every =use= — the wrapper loss (=timeout=) is documented, not fixed.
- *F4 =/status= semantics*: pulse (timestamp per request) or boot stamp (rename to =started=)?
  Recommendation: both fields — =started= + =timestamp=; the README's word "pulse" then holds.
- *F5 what the supervisor forwards*: the 15-line banner is honest but noisy — trim the SvelteKit
  deprecations at the source (kajuit config) rather than in the README?

* taskbag — ghost process management

beef, verbatim: /"how to fix? whats the right approach? we want coherent process management. i feel
like there are issues here all over. collect a taskbag here."/

** the surface today (measured, read-only)

- =subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/ledger.js= — =spawn= · =boot= · =kill= · =lock= · =locks= · =log= · =arm= · =teardown=; =attached= is a per-process Set.
- =subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/process.js= — =Deno.Command= + lock write at spawn; =child.status.then(lock.remove)=; =unref()= for detached; =Pipe= for piped; =kill()= = SIGTERM then =await status=.
- =subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/lock.js= — JSON =<instance>_<process>.lock=, =alive()= via =SIGURG=.
- =subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/log.js= — =logs/<instance>/<process>.<stream>.log= — *zero callers* (=grep ledger\.log(= → only a comment in =mod.js:24=).
- =systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/{run,start,stop,init}.js= + =target.js specs()= — the four verbs, each with its own idea of "the instance" (=register()= writes, =lookup()= reads).
- =systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/Init.jsx= — the ONLY supervisor UI; readiness = =line.includes("Status:ALIVE")=.
- Prior art: =known-issues.org= =detached-process-die-on-parent-exit= (=unref()= ≠ detach, macOS measured, four options none taken).

** the bag

| id  | locus                                            | defect                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | shape of fix                                                                                      |
|-----+--------------------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| B1  | =Init.jsx:37-46=                                 | =ready= settles ONLY on =Status:ALIVE=; =process.status= is tapped into =exits= state but never rejects the wait → "starting processes…" forever when any child dies (L5)                                                     | =ready= = =Promise.race([alive, status.then(exit => reject(exit))])=; phase =failed= shows tail + codes; exit 1 |
| B2  | =Init.jsx:41=, =:133=                            | =process.spec.type= does not exist — =specs()= carries =process=; the tail prefix prints =undefined=, the task labels are empty (L16)                                                                                        | read =spec.process=                                                                               |
| B3  | =run.js:19-22= + =mod.js:178-181=                | child exit ≠ ghost exit: =instance/run= whose runtime dies with code 1 exits 0 — =effect.children[].code= is data, never a fault; the exit site only knows =ctx.error= / =effect.error=                                       | fold child codes into the effect's error (=exit 1= iff any child =!success=)                     |
| B4  | =process.js:18,:39= + known issue                | =detached= = stdout/stderr =null= + =unref()=: the children have NO log and die with the parent (macOS); =Log= prototype, the =logs/= scaffold and doctor's =collectLogs= read a dir nothing writes                            | attachment decides WHERE output goes, never WHETHER it is kept: detached → =Log.open()=; true detach needs the setsid shim (known issue) |
| B5  | =process.js:38= + =stop.js:11= + =instances/index.js:55= | lock removal rides =child.status.then= — alive only in the ghost that spawned; a detached child's lock outlives it. =start= compensates (=alive()= + =remove=, =start.js:9-13=); =stop= SIGTERMs whatever pid the lock holds (recycled pid risk); =instances/list= "running" comes from a dead lock | liveness at READ: =ledger.locks()= prunes dead pids — one reader for start/stop/list/doctor      |
| B6  | =process.js:56-67=                               | =kill()= = SIGTERM then =await status= with no deadline; a child that ignores SIGTERM hangs teardown (and =Ledger.teardown=, and =Init= "enter → stop") forever; SIGKILL only on a throw                                     | SIGTERM → deadline → SIGKILL, one policy                                                           |
| B7  | =runtime/lifecycle/integrate.js:22,:47= · =kajuit/vite.config.mjs:101= · =Init.jsx:42= | readiness is a string scrape: three producers (=Status("alive")= at listen, =$[runtime:slug] {status}= every patrol, the vite beacon), one substring consumer, no timeout, no structured channel — L21's three-shaped banner is this defect seen from the README | one readiness signal per process (a line the Process recognises, or a probe of =/status=), with a deadline |
| B8  | =ledger.js:73-84= + =run.js= (=inherit=)         | =arm()= teardown exits 0 on SIGTERM/SIGQUIT regardless of children; under =inherit= the children share the tty process group → they get the operator's SIGINT AND ghost's SIGTERM                                            | exit code from the fold (B3); signal children once                                                |
| B9  | =init.js:51-62=                                  | headless =poll()= = 30 × 1 s blind retries; a dead runtime costs 30 s and reports "Network request failed", never "runtime exited 1" — B1's twin                                                                             | poll races the same =ready=/=exited= settlement                                                   |
| B10 | =ledger/index.js:130-137=                        | doctor =processes.attached= reports the doctor's OWN =attached= set — always empty, every =viva= is a fresh process                                                                                                          | drop the field; processes = locks with liveness (B5)                                              |
| B11 | (no verb)                                        | nothing answers "what is running / what did it print": =instances/list= running-column from locks (B5), =/status= by hand-curl, no =instance/logs=, no =instance/status=                                                     | =instance/status= (locks + liveness + =/status= probe) · =instance/logs= (tail of B4's files)     |

** the right approach — one stance, then the bag drains

A Process is a *state machine* owned by the ledger — =spawned → ready | exited(code)= — not a bag of
promises that two verbs read differently. Readiness and exit are two settlements of ONE promise:
=process.ready= resolves on the readiness signal and REJECTS on exit, with a deadline. Attachment is a
policy for where stdout goes (tty / pipe / log file), never for whether it is kept. Locks carry
liveness at read, one reader. Ghost's exit code is the fold of its children's. Then =Init.jsx=,
=run.js= and the headless =init= path all consume the same =ready=/=exited= and the hang class (B1,
B9), the silent-zero class (B3, B8) and the stale-lock class (B5, B10) cannot exist.

Order: B1+B2 (S, unblocks the README) → B3+B8 (S) → B5+B10+B6 (S, one liveness reader) → B7 (M,
readiness contract) → B4+B11+B13 (M, logs + status verbs) → detach shim (separate, known issue).
Tests first: =init.test.js= (fake process exits 1 → terminal phase), =run= exit-code test, =locks()=
prune test.

** L5 — the diffs, unapplied (beef: /"add l5 to the taskbag"/)

is the setsid shim from =known-issues.org=, not these diffs.

** the right approach

ONE resolver — =select(token) → { slug, mount, provenance }= — that flag, cwd, session, ledger,
=doctor <target>= and =create --use= all call; the record is the only slug reader, =path.pin= the
only path reader; flags hoisted and parsed once at the door; chaining a dispatcher rule with error
propagation; sessions owned by the resolver (reap on write); one report shape. C3, C4, C6, C7 are
XS each and can land now; C2 + C8 + C9 are the resolver (M); C5 waits on F3; C11/C12 need a
measurement first.

* the connoisseur's pass — process management + the use/--instance cluster

beef, verbatim: /"something is off architecturally. things are not as coherent and well layed out as
the rest of the system. let the coneusseur have a pass. the problems are plenty. scope is large. and
i the code is vibed. time for adult supervision."/

Read against =self/connoisseur.md=: one structure, one law, made visible. Every path:line below was
re-read this pass; every claim marked /measured/ was run.

** the verdict, in english

The coherent parts of this system each have one structure and one law. =Env= is an ordered map of
strata and first-hit lookup (=env.js:62-67=). =scope= is a map of =[condition, resolver]= and a
Proxy that asks the condition (=scope.js:14-40=). The runtime is a =Wafer= with a =Status= atom, an
abort, and five lifecycle verbs, and its =Die= supervises its daemons and processes with exactly
those verbs (=systems/runtime/die.js:23-28,43-53,55-73=). Neither taskbag names a structure. They
name twenty-five symptoms of two absences.

*Processes.* =Ledger= is two objects wearing one name. It is the record — =instances.json=,
=registry.json=, =locks/=, =sessions/=, =logs/= — and it is also a live supervisor: an =attached=
Set, =arm()=, =teardown()=, =spawn/boot/kill= (=ledger.js:10-11,46-84=). Files cannot supervise.
The only truth about a pid is the kernel's table; a lock file is a claim against it, and today the
claim is read as truth by =instances/list= and =stop= while =start= re-verifies it by hand
(=start.js:9-13=). Readiness is a substring scrape (=Init.jsx:42=) because no object owns "ready";
exit never rejects (=Init.jsx:37-46=) because no object owns "exited"; =run= exits 0 on a dead child
(=run.js:19-22=) because nothing folds the children into the parent. The object that owns all four
already exists: a spawned child is a =Wafer=. populate = the command, resolve = spawn + lock,
integrate = ready-or-throw, perpetuate = await exit, disintegrate = SIGTERM → grace → SIGKILL. The
set of children is a =Die= over those wafers, the same shape =systems/runtime/die.js= already is.
Then =run=, =init= and the wizard are consumers of one lifecycle and the hang class, the silent-zero
class and the stale-lock class have nowhere to live. The readiness line itself is already a
structured value on the producer side — =console.log(new Status("alive"))= at =integrate.js:47= and
=vite.config.mjs:100= prints through the inspect symbol (=status.js:48-50=). The consumer scrapes it
because the pair was never named: =Status → line= exists, =line → Status= does not.

*Detached.* =start= without a living owner is a category error, not a bug. =unref()= was never
detach; the known issue proposed four shims for it. Measured this pass: =Deno.Command= carries
=detached: true= (deno 2.9.6, =deno types= :4210 — /"allows the spawned process to continue running
after the current process exits"/); the probe child got its own process group (pgid = its pid) and
outlived its parent. The shim fork is dead. With it, =start= is =run= spawned detached with its
output logged — the ghost is the supervisor in every verb, and the one lock is the supervisor's own
pid, so a lock is alive exactly when its writer is.

*Selection.* One pair is unnamed: /reference ⟷ mount/. The cata exists —
=instances.lookup(mount) → slug= (=instances.js:25-29=). The ana is written six times: the shelf
guess (=path.js:18-21=), the record-or-stat guess (=doctor.js:22-29=), =use='s local branch
(=use.js:15-16=), =create='s pin-or-accio (=create.js:23-31=), the slug throw in the scope resolver
(=populate.js:41-45=), and the mounts loop at the door (=mod.js:140-146=). And =populate.js:40=
says so in its own voice: /"the CLI resolves a slug via ledger.instances.resolve() before writing"/.
Measured: no =resolve= exists anywhere. The code knew the method it needed; every site wrote a
private one instead. Write it once, delete five; =shelf= survives only as =create='s default
destination, where =create.js:40= currently rebuilds it by hand.

*The session.* Your paste is the proof. =~/.viva/.env= holds no =VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT=;
=sessions/27586.json= and =28293.json= — both live zsh — hold =hello-world=; =deno task
runtime/watch= has no =VIVA_PROCESS_ID=, so =populate.js:83= skips the session stratum and
=instance.js:208= throws. The session is keyed by a number only the wrapper script can mint
(=ghost.sh:8=), invisible to =deno task=, =sudo=, =$(…)=, and reaped only by a doctor verb. Write at
the owner: what the shell and every process it launches share is the terminal. Key the session by
the tty and =viva instances/use x= and =deno task runtime/watch= in the same terminal agree, =ghost.sh=
loses four lines, and no reaper exists — a session is dead when its device is. One measurement is
owed (this harness has no pty): =! deno run -A /Users/finn/.claude/jobs/59b6624d/tmp/tty.js= in your
terminal, paste the two lines.

*The door.* Flags are parsed at three doors — the coercion for tokens after the nature
(=shellsignal.js:17-28=), =--help= alone at =mod.js:127-128=, the mounts and =--env= at
=mod.js:140-161= — and described at a fourth (=trajectories/index.js:10-16=). One table, one parse.
=ctx.call= returns =context.effect= and drops =inner.error= (=mod.js:63-68=, =strategy= :11-17):
chaining swallows failure by construction. The cwd stratum mounts eagerly for =ctx.instance=
(=mod.js:78=) — measured: one writer, zero readers.

** measured this pass

| fact                                                        | evidence                                                                                              |
|-------------------------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| =Deno.Command= has =detached: true=                         | =deno types= :4210, deno 2.9.6; probe: =sleep= child pgid = own pid, alive after parent exit          |
| without it the child shares the parent's group              | same probe, =detached: false=: pgid = parent's                                                        |
| =Deno.ppid= exists                                          | probe printed it; =ghost.sh:8= exports what the runtime already knows                                 |
| beef's =nothing selected= = session stratum, not ledger     | =~/.viva/.env= no mount; two live =sessions/<zsh pid>.json= hold hello-world; runtime has no PROCESS_ID |

| tty as session key                                          | =Deno.stat("/dev/ttys000")= exposes =rdev=; the =/dev/stdin= route UNMEASURED (no pty here)           |

** cluster A — the child is a Wafer

*** triggers firing

- #4 self-priming — twin: =new Process(ledger, spec).spawn()= (=ledger.js:48=), =arm()= as a =.start()= (=ledger.js:73-79=).
- #5 multimethod on a live value — twin: =attachment= frozen into a table at construction (=process.js:18=) and re-branched at =:25,:39,:40= and =ledger.js:49=.
- #10 totality, LOUD — =ready= only resolves; =kill()= swallows into =/* gone */= (=process.js:60-66=); =locks()= =catch { return [] }= (=ledger.js:41-43=); =run= exits 0 on =!exit.success=.
- #15 write at the owner — the lock is written by the spawner and removed by =child.status.then= (=process.js:38=), which only runs if the spawner outlives the child; liveness is re-derived by three readers or not at all.
- #18 the bag — =spec= = ={process, instance, mount, attachment, cmd, env, clearEnv, cwd}= (=target.js:29-36=): identity, command and policy in one object; =specs(param, {attachment, instance})= a second bag.
- #3 one core, thin cases — =run/start/stop/init= each own a copy of "resolve, spec, spawn, wait"; =init= owns two (headless =poll= 30×1 s, wizard scrape).
- #16 no second epicycle — =unref()= is not detach → =start= pre-cleans locks → =stop= kills the lock's pid → =list= trusts the lock: three orbits around one absent flag.
- #19 grepped, never remembered — the known issue's four shims for =detached: true=.
- #7 cata/ana — =Status → "Status:ALIVE"= (=status.js:48-50=) has no inverse; =Init.jsx:42= is the missing =parse= written as =includes=.

*** the shape

=subsystems/typology/prototypes/status.js= — the inverse of the inspector. TYPOLOGY: gated, ask first.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
  static parse(line) {
    const match = /^Status:([A-Z_<>]+)$/.exec(String(line).trim());
    return match && new Status(match[1]);
  }
#+END_SRC

=subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/process.js= — the whole file. =Wafer= is =subsystems/typology/prototypes/wafer.js:3-27=.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
import { Pipe, Queue, Status, Wafer } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { TextLineStream } from "@std/streams";

const lines = (stream) => stream.pipeThrough(new TextDecoderStream()).pipeThrough(new TextLineStream());
const STDIO = { inherit: "inherit", piped: "piped", logged: "piped" };

export class Process extends Wafer {
  in = new Queue();
  out = new Pipe();

  get manifest() {
    return { type: "process", slug: this.identity.process };
  }

  async populate() {
    this.status.set("populated");
  }

  async resolve() {
    const std = STDIO[this.attachment];
    this.child = new Deno.Command(this.command.bin, {
      args: this.command.args,
      cwd: this.command.cwd,
      env: this.command.env,
      clearEnv: true,
      stdin: std === "piped" ? "piped" : "null",
      stdout: std,
      stderr: std,
    }).spawn();
    if (std === "piped") {
      this.in.to(this.child.stdin);
      this.out.from(lines(this.child.stdout), lines(this.child.stderr));
    }
    if (this.attachment === "logged") {
      this.out.to(await this.ledger.log(this.identity.instance).open(this.identity.process, "out"));
    }
    this.child.status.then((exit) => this.status.set({ code: "exited", exit }));
    this.status.set({ code: "spawned", pid: this.child.pid });
  }

  async integrate() {
    const deadline = this.command.deadline ?? 60_000;
    const alive = this.attachment === "inherit"
      ? Promise.resolve()
      : new Promise((resolve) => this.out.tap((line) => Status.parse(line)?.is("alive") && resolve()));
    const exited = this.child.status.then((exit) => {
      throw Object.assign(new Error(`${this.slug} exited ${exit.code}`), { exit });
    });
    const late = new Promise((_, reject) =>
      setTimeout(reject, deadline, new Error(`${this.slug} not ready after ${deadline} ms`))
    );
    await Promise.race([alive, exited, late]);
    this.status.set("alive");
  }

  async perpetuate() {
    return await this.child.status;
  }

  async disintegrate() {
    if (this.status.is(["exited", "stopping"])) return;
    this.status.set("stopping");
    try {
      this.child.kill("SIGTERM");
    } catch {
      return;
    }
    const grace = new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, this.command.grace ?? 5_000, "grace"));
    if ((await Promise.race([this.child.status, grace])) === "grace") this.child.kill("SIGKILL");
    await this.child.status;
  }
}
#+END_SRC

=subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/die.js= — NEW. The set of children; the same shape as =systems/runtime/die.js=. ONE lock per die, written by the die, keyed by its own pid.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
import { Wafer } from "@vivalence/typology";
import { Process } from "./process.js";

export class Die extends Wafer {
  get manifest() {
    return { type: "instance", slug: this.instance };
  }

  async populate() {
    this.good = { processes: this.specs.map((spec) => new Process({ ledger: this.ledger, ...spec })) };
    this.lock = this.ledger.lock(this.instance);
    for (const process of this.good.processes) await process.populate();
  }

  async resolve() {
    for (const process of this.good.processes) await process.resolve();
    await this.lock.write({
      pid: Deno.pid,
      instance: this.instance,
      processes: this.good.processes.map((process) => ({ process: process.slug, pid: process.child.pid })),
      started: new Date().toISOString(),
    });
  }

  async integrate() {
    await Promise.all(this.good.processes.map((process) => process.integrate()));
    this.status.set("alive");
  }

  async perpetuate() {
    for (const signal of ["SIGINT", "SIGTERM", "SIGQUIT"]) Deno.addSignalListener(signal, () => this.disintegrate());
    const exits = await Promise.all(this.good.processes.map((process) => process.perpetuate()));
    await this.lock.remove();
    this.status.set({ code: "exited", exits });
    return exits;
  }

  async disintegrate() {
    if (this.status.is(["stopping", "exited"])) return;
    this.status.set("stopping");
    await Promise.all(this.good.processes.map((process) => process.disintegrate()));
    await this.lock.remove();
    this.abort.abort();
  }
}
#+END_SRC

=subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/ledger.js= — the record sheds its live state. =attached=, =armed=, =arm=, =teardown=, =spawn=, =kill= go; =boot= returns a live die.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
  lock(instance) {
    return new Lock(this.paladin, this.paladin.scope.ledger.branch(`/locks/${instance}.lock`));
  }

  async boot(specs, { instance, attachment = "inherit" }) {
    this.paladin.publish();
    const die = new Die({ ledger: this, specs, instance, attachment });
    await die.populate();
    await die.resolve();
    try {
      await die.integrate();
    } catch (error) {
      await die.disintegrate();
      throw error;
    }
    return die;
  }
#+END_SRC

=subsystems/paladin/prototypes/ledger/lock.js= — a lock whose pid is dead is not a lock. =read= IS =alive=; =alive()= goes.

    const lock = await this.paladin.read.json(this.path, null);
    if (!lock) return null;
    try {
      Deno.kill(lock.pid, "SIGURG");
      return lock;
    } catch {

=systems/ghost/trajectories/instance/target.js= — the bag split into identity · command · attachment; the child's env is explicit (B13).

#+BEGIN_SRC js
const INHERITED = ["PATH", "HOME", "XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "TERM", "LANG", "DENO_DIR"];

export function specs(param) {
  const target = param ?? "all";
  const known = ["all", ...Object.keys(CHILDREN)];
  if (!known.includes(target)) throw new Error(`instance: unknown target '${target}' — expected ${known.join(" | ")}`);
  const mount = paladin.instance.home.absolute;
  const config = `${paladin.scope.repository.absolute}/deno.jsonc`;
  const env = {
    ...object.filter(Deno.env.toObject(), (key) => INHERITED.includes(key)),
    VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT: mount,
    VIVA_LEDGER_MOUNT: paladin.scope.ledger.absolute,
    VIVA_REPOSITORY_MOUNT: paladin.scope.repository.absolute,
  };
  return (target === "all" ? Object.keys(CHILDREN) : [target]).map((process) => ({
    identity: { process, mount },
    command: { bin: "deno", args: ["task", "--config", config, "-q", CHILDREN[process].task], env },
  }));
}
#+END_SRC

The four verbs become thin cases over one lifecycle.

#+BEGIN_SRC js
export async function run(ctx) {
  const instance = await register();
  const die = await paladin.ledger.boot(specs(ctx.signal.params[0]), { instance, attachment: ctx.signal.flags?.logged ? "logged" : "inherit" });
  const exits = await die.perpetuate();
  const failed = exits.filter((exit) => !exit.success);
  ctx.effect = { status: failed.length ? "failed" : "ran", instance, children: exits };
  if (failed.length) throw new Error(`instance: ${failed.map((exit, at) => `${die.good.processes[at].slug} exited ${exit.code}`).join(", ")}`);
}

export async function start(ctx) {
  const instance = await register();
  if (await paladin.ledger.lock(instance).read()) throw new Error(`${instance} already running — viva instance/stop`);
  const supervisor = new Deno.Command(Deno.execPath(), {
    args: ["run", "-A", new URL("../../mod.js", import.meta.url).pathname, "instance/run", ...(ctx.signal.params[0] ? [ctx.signal.params[0]] : []), `--instance=${paladin.instance.home.absolute}`, "--logged"],
    detached: true,
    stdin: "null",
    stdout: "null",
    stderr: "null",
  }).spawn();
  supervisor.unref();
  ctx.effect = { status: "started", instance, supervisor: supervisor.pid, logs: paladin.scope.ledger.branch(`logs/${instance}`).absolute };
}

export async function stop(ctx) {
  const held = await paladin.ledger.instances.lookup(paladin.instance.home.absolute);
  const lock = await paladin.ledger.lock(held.slug).read();
  if (!lock) return (ctx.effect = { status: "nothing-to-stop", instance: held.slug });
  Deno.kill(lock.pid, "SIGTERM");
  ctx.effect = { status: "stopped", instance: held.slug, supervisor: lock.pid, processes: lock.processes };
}
#+END_SRC

export const NOTHING =
  "instance: nothing selected — viva instances/use <slug|path>, --instance=<slug|path>, or VIVA_INSTANCE_MOUNT=<path>";

  async resolve(reference) {
    if (!reference) throw new Error(NOTHING);
    const local = reference.includes("/") || reference.startsWith(".");
    if (local) {
      const mount = this.paladin.source(reference).absolute;
      const held = await this.lookup(mount);
      if (!held) throw new Error(`instance: ${mount} is not recorded — viva instances/tap ${reference} --slug=<slug>`);
      return held;
    }
    const held = await this.read(reference);
    if (!held) throw new Error(`instance: no record '${reference}' — viva instances/list`);
    return { slug: reference, ...held };
  }
#+END_SRC
